# Soundstage changelog

Soundstage is updated continuously on `main`. Newest first. Times are US Pacific.
Each entry links to the code; every milestone ships with tests.

## 2026-11-08

**Feet, fifth pass: the fourth pass's regressions fixed (shoes through each other along an edge, a kerb's 15 cm slides, stairs taken as jumps), the ramp "float" disproved in the browser, toe-off slides (private branch `private/feet`, 03:10 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked in Node on the real Rocketbox files and re-recorded in a headless browser (the try-it build of this branch on a local server, every frame through `setFrame`); no GPU render, nobody has watched it moving. **Not merge-ready on the lane rule:** `web/stage/stage.mjs`, `web/stage/people/footplant.mjs` (and the earlier `avatars.mjs` change) are the other agent's lane (CLAUDE.md) and their OK has not been asked or given. Moving off a break beside the body moves the *whole body* up to 20 cm sideways (keyed 2 cm off a platform's edge the person walks a hand's width from where they were keyed; the clay, its shadow and bounding box are not moved). Slides still over 1 cm: the man at the foot of the stairs (1.04 cm, unchanged since 3abc644), a 720 degree spin in half a second (1.56 man, 1.77 woman, unchanged), the man over a sharp 30 degree ridge (1.04), the woman running down stairs at 3 m/s (2.78, the same on 854e956). Over the 30 degree ridge the woman has 6 frames with no foot down at the apex (854e956: 14), and the trailing leg on each side dips the hips up to 6 cm. The tall-person hip dip on a 10 degree run (2.4 m: 3.74 -> 5.57 cm) is kept: 854e956's smaller dip came from the body sitting 8.3 cm under the slope. In the browser the woman's shoes come within 2.2 mm in the walk into a stop and the man's within 2.5 mm in the platform U-turn (the clip's own feet meeting in a turn; 8 mm on the flat in Node). Pre-existing and not touched: walking beside a 60 cm box puts a shoe 8-12 cm into it (the body's footing reads the box's top within 18 cm); a 7.5 cm kerb walked 3 cm onto has a 6 cm hip dip and 1.6 cm slide; stairs walked down from a landing at 1.2 m/s float up to 23 frames. The kerb and box cases are Node only (no kerb in a browser set).
- **Shoes through each other (severe, the fourth pass):** one planted shoe across a break was moved up to 23 cm sideways onto the other foot's line: along the station edge (x 1.58-1.70), a 15 cm kerb and a 1 m dock the shoes came within 1.7-4.7 mm and passed through each other, the foot bones' side gap 14 cm -> 0.03-1.2 cm (a tightrope walk). Now the whole body moves (`plan.shift`, per frame, held over half a stride and eased; [web/stage/people/footplant.mjs](web/stage/people/footplant.mjs) `planFeet`, `groundFeet` moves the root bone), only for a break that runs along the way (a step up onto a crate is stepped onto, not walked round), and a foot swinging low counts too: closest shoes as on the flat (man 4.95-6.5 cm, woman 1.8-2.3 cm; flat 5.8-6.1 / 0.8), foot bones 9.2-14 cm apart passing. The per-shoe `T.straddle` search and `plan.dS` are gone.
- **Kerb's low side and a box beside:** the woman keyed 3-6 cm off a 15 cm kerb's face slid 12.2-14.7 cm a frame with a shoe 0.5-0.7 cm into the kerb; beside a 20 cm box at x 1.04 a shoe went 0.8 cm in. Now 0.10-0.43 cm slides, 0 cm inside (man and woman, 20 and 40 cm boxes).
- **Stairs as jumps:** the jump check summed every break within a quarter second against the leg-scaled limit, so two 17 cm risers were a jump for anyone 1.3 m or shorter (the library child: frames 57-59 hidden, the body 16 cm over the treads), the woman running up 15 cm stairs at 2.5 m/s hid 11 frames (854e956: 6) and a run down keyed in a straight line 7 (854e956: 0). Each break is judged on its own (looks that break one after another are one break), and a walked step spreads over at most 50 cm of travel (`STEP_SPAN`; a quarter second at 2.5 m/s is 62 cm: the body lagged 15 cm under the tread and a swinging shoe went 2.9 cm into the next riser). 0 frames hidden in every case ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs) `_walkTable`).
- **Smaller ones:** over a sharp 30 degree ridge the body followed the apex exactly, 5.8 cm/frame^2 there (a pop); the smooth part of the footing is now eased over 12 cm of travel either way (`RIDGE_EASE`): 2.5 cm/frame^2, 2 cm under the apex (854e956: 2.3 but 9 cm under it). The woman's 2.9 cm slide there was a plant ending out of reach, its blend weighted straight to the clip's foot; a lone walking foot's blend may now dip the hips like a support frame (0.89 cm). Walking down off a 40 cm box the toe out over the drop was pinned as the contact while the sole on the edge slid 1.67 cm; the pinned patch is now the sole nearest the ground UNDER it (0.47 cm; the man 1.66 -> 0.94). Toe-off: a walking stance lasts up to 2 more frames while the sole is still down on level ground (`T.toeOff`), so the 2.1 m man's 1.01 cm slide is 0.37 and his kerb 0.42.
- **The ramp "float" (the done bar):** the browser recorder measured the LOWEST shoe vertex against the ground under that vertex; on a slope that is the heel lifting at toe-off. Re-recorded with every vertex against the ground under it: the planted shoes are 0.0 mm off the ramp on every planted frame (man and woman), while the old number on the same frames reads 20.6 / 15.8 mm. Other browser numbers (max, not median): platform U-turn slide 5.2 mm (was 11.4), walk into a stop 2.5 mm (was a 10.2 mm slip) with a 12.5 mm hip dip, ramp slides 9.2 / 1.8 mm, the station edge walk 0 floating frames and the shoes 16-58 mm apart at the closest; the woman's "17.9 mm hanging foot" in the 360 turn is the first frame of a turn step lifting on its arc (17.9 -> 47 -> 17.9 mm, state stepping).
- **Corrected claim:** the fourth pass said the stop / U-turn hip dip went from 5 cm on 854e956 to 1.3 cm. 854e956 already measured 1.28-1.71 cm; the test is now labelled a guard on older behaviour and held at 2 cm.
- **Tests** ([tests/js/footground.test.mjs](tests/js/footground.test.mjs)): new: the closest left/right shoe vertices and the foot gap along the station edge, a kerb and a dock; the kerb's low side and a box beside; 17 / 30 stairs for 1.1-1.3 m people and the library child; stairs run up and down; the ridge and walking down off a 40 cm box; toe-off for a 2.1 m man. All six fail on df4288b. Full suites: JS 1673 tests, 1671 pass, 0 fail (1 skipped, 1 todo); Python 1807 passed, 25 skipped. Knowledge pack rebuilt (unchanged).

**Feet, fourth pass: slopes followed exactly at any speed and frame rate (the downhill float Mike saw), the step limit scales with the leg, platform edges, the ramp "float" reconciled, no test hang (private branch `private/feet`, 02:19 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked in Node only on the real Rocketbox files, no new browser recording, no GPU render, nobody has watched it moving. The clip's stride is a flat-ground one: on a 10-20 degree slope the trailing leg still dips the hips 4-6 cm (the 6 cm support cap), and running uphill floats more than on the flat (60 floating frames in 121 for the woman at 4-6 m/s up 20 degrees, 39-46 on the flat; each flight at most 7 frames, the flat's 6). At 12 fps the woman sprinting up 20 degrees (6 m/s on average) has a 6-frame flight, half a second. Slides still over the 1 cm bar: the 2.1 m man walking (1.09 cm, was 1.29) and on a kerb (1.11), the man at the foot of the stairs (1.04, unchanged), the woman's 720 degrees in half a second (1.77, unchanged); all are the toe still touching on the first blend-out frame after a plant. A planted top up to half the leg is now allowed (below), and walking down off a 40 cm box the woman's trailing toe on the box edge slides 1.67 cm on its last frame (was 0.49). Keyed 2-4 cm off a platform's edge the shoes stay out of its face now, but only by moving the planted foot up to 20 cm sideways (a narrower, slightly crossed step): the keys are still closer to the wall than a body fits. The U-turn on the platform measured 1.14 cm in the browser before; in Node the same kind of U-turn is 0.48-0.56 cm, the browser case was not re-recorded. The 2-4 cm hip dip into a stop is 1.3 cm in Node (the stop-go test now holds it under 3 cm), not re-checked in the browser. *(Corrected in the fifth pass: 1.28-1.71 cm is what 854e956 already measured; this pass did not change it, and the test is a guard on older behaviour.)* The tryit_edges "re-publish the try-it demo" notice (chase, drycreek, fire, western scenes) is a TODO, not from this branch. `web/stage/**` is the other lane's (CLAUDE.md): `stage.mjs`, `avatars.mjs` and `footplant.mjs` changes need their OK before merge.
- **Slopes (the regression, worse than 3abc644):** `_walkTable` took any footing change over 2.5 cm from one FRAME to the next as a step, so a slope walked fast enough (20 degrees at 1.6 m/s, 10 at 3.4) was a run of steps averaged over a quarter second: downhill the body floated 11-23 cm and a foot hung 17-26 cm up, uphill the body sank 8-22 cm, 4-6 m/s up 20 degrees was a jump (21-97 frames hidden), and the answer changed with the frame rate. Now a step is a break in the ground itself: between two frames whose footing differs by over 2.5 cm the path is looked at every 2 cm and only a change over 2.5 cm between two looks is a step; jumps are those breaks (plus the keyed height) over the step limit within a quarter second ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs) `_walkTable`, `STEP_PROBE`). The body is within 0.01 cm of the true slope on every frame of 48 cases (10 / 20 degrees, 2 / 4 / 6 m/s, up and down, man and woman, 24 and 12 fps; 854e956: 2-38 cm off); downhill floating frames 56-75 -> 36-42 (the flat run's 31-38), a foot up 17-26 cm -> the flight's 3-6 frames. The woman's 2 floating frames on the 20 degree hill walk are 0.
- **Found by the new slope test and fixed:** at 12 fps a 2-frame plant (as long as 3 at 24 fps) was dropped, so a run up 20 degrees had no foot down for 6 frames (`planFeet` scales `T.minStance` by fps); and a foot at rest was kept clear of a LEVEL footing, so at the end of a shot up a 10 degree hill the front shoe stood 4.7 cm into it (now the footing laid along the ground's slope at the body, the smaller one-sided difference, so a ledge beside the body is still no slope: `plan.slope`).
- **The station ramp "float" (16.6-17.2 mm in the browser re-record) reconciled:** played in Node with the recording's own ids and keys (120 frames, 1.73 m/s up the ramp onto the platform), the foot bones land within 0.1 mm of the recorded ones and the recorder's numbers come back frame for frame (man f53 17.2 mm, woman f118 16.6 mm). They are the recorder's measure, not a gap: the LOWEST shoe vertex minus the ramp under THAT vertex. Late in a stance the heel is up and the ball touches 7-14 cm further up the 6.6 degree ramp, so the lowest vertex sits 8-16 mm over lower ramp while the touching one is at 0 mm, on every planted frame. The earlier harness measured the nearest vertex to the ground (0 mm), which is why it "could not reproduce" it; nothing differed in mode or frame. Pinned in the ramp test.
- **Step limit by leg length:** a rise or drop up to half the leg (a quarter of the height) is a walked step, more is a jump; a planted top up to half the leg from the body's footing is planted (`T.far` 0.3 -> 0.5; it was under the walked step, so a top the body walked up was refused). 1.1-1.4 m people at a 40 cm box: shoe 9.5-17 cm inside it -> 0 (they jump it); a 1.1 m man on a 20 cm kerb: 6.7 cm in, 5.1 cm/frame slide -> 0, 0.6; the 1.8 m man on a 40 cm box: 8.35 cm/frame slide on top -> 0.34.
- **Platform edges:** keyed 2-4 cm off the edge (rail level) one shoe was 9.5-13 cm inside the platform's face -> 0-0.7 cm; 2 cm in from it the outer shoe hung over the drop, 38 floating frames -> 0; keyed exactly on the edge (x 1.62) the interpolated key was 1e-15 off the platform every other frame and the body fell 92 cm and back (20 frames in the air, 17-27 cm/frame slides) -> on the platform, slides 0.1-0.4 cm. A planted shoe across a break over 10 cm from the body's footing moves across the heading, 2 cm at a time up to 20 cm, to the first place with the whole sole on the body's ground (plus 3 cm), and the swing follows it (`T.straddle`, `T.sideMax`, `plan.dS`); the body's footing looks at the set floor 2 mm either way (`EDGE_EPS`).
- **Tests and the stall:** the after-drag poll of a live drag reschedules itself every 120 ms; footplant.test ended its drag only after an assert, so a failing assert left it on and the node process never exited (the likely 37-minute stall of a combined footground + footplant run). Now the drag ends in a `finally` and the timer is unref'd in Node ([web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs)); footground + footplant together exit in 16 s. Key edit cost unchanged (24 ms for 5 s, 115 ms for 30 s). New in [tests/js/footground.test.mjs](tests/js/footground.test.mjs): the slope matrix against the TRUE ground (body height, every planted sole, no hidden frame, no slope taken as a jump, the flight), people of 1.1 / 1.4 / 1.8 m at a 20 / 40 cm box, walking along the platform edge at x 1.58-1.64, the recorded ramp walk; all three new physical tests fail on 854e956. Tightened: the 20 degree hill allows 0 floating frames (was 2), the stop and U-turn hip dip 3 cm (was 5.1; *corrected in the fifth pass: the 5 cm dip was not on 854e956, which already measured 1.28-1.71 cm, so this tightening guarded nothing this pass changed*).

**Feet, third pass: no floating at a stop, a restart or a U-turn, feet stay on the ground beside a platform or a ledge, walked step-ups, every sprint stride touches down, the swim gate per frame, key edits 2x faster (private branch `private/feet`, 00:36 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** the woman's last step into a stop waits planted with a 5 cm single-support hip dip for 3 frames; the trailing leg on a 20 degree hill dips the hips 5.5-6 cm (the clip's stride is a flat-ground one) and the woman still has 2 frames with no foot within 5 mm there; a 40 cm set-box step walked at 1 m/s catches the man's toe (2.2 cm into it, 2.3 cm slide); slides still over the 1 cm bar: the 2.1 m man (1.29 cm walking, 1.13 cm on the kerb), the man's U-turn on the platform in the browser (1.14 cm, the same at 3abc644), the man at the foot of the stairs (1.04 cm); from a standing start the man's trailing foot drags for ~10 frames (no plant before it); through 2-3 m/s (the walk-to-run blend) the woman has a 5-6 frame flight. Keyed 2 cm off a ledge or the platform wall, the toes or heels go into its side (the keys put the body closer than the shoe). A 30 s shot still costs ~115 ms per key edit, so during a live drag a shot over 240 frames shows the clip's own feet until the drop. The station ramp's 16.9 mm float from the earlier browser recording was **not reproduced**: in a real browser (try-it page, the real Stage and station set, every frame through `setFrame`) a walk up the ramp has planted feet within 0 mm and 0 floating frames at 3abc644 as well as now; the ramp floats measured then were most likely the walk-stop (4 frames, fixed here) or run speeds. The clay's own height table, swap rules and hops are untouched; `web/stage/**` is the other agent's lane (CLAUDE.md) and `stage.mjs` changes here need their OK before merge; nothing in `sets/train.mjs` changed.
- **Floating at a stop, a restart and U-turns (the regression Mike saw):** the idle-walk crossfade drags both feet at once, and 3abc644 stepped them together with the swing clearance holding both 3.0 cm up: 4-8 frames with no foot down. Walking (under 2 m/s), a step now waits planted for the other foot, lands early and waits, or the other foot waits; any frame left with both feet off is closed by a hold ([web/stage/people/footplant.mjs](web/stage/people/footplant.mjs) `planFeet`). Browser, platform: walk-stop-go 7 floating frames -> 0 (man and woman), U-turn 5-8 -> 0, ramp walk-then-stop 4 -> 0 (lower foot 28-30 mm up -> 0).
- **Legs folded onto a top beside the body** (severe, pre-existing): the clearance pushed a foot onto any top under a sole point with no limit, and the reach guard only refused targets too low. Browser, standing at x 1.60 (the platform edge at 1.62): the foot bone at 1.021 m -> 0.101 m over the body's floor. Now the clearance counts only ground the leg could step onto (none over the body's footing for a foot at rest, 30 cm for a swing, up to the higher of the two plants it swings between) and never folds the leg closer than 45 % of its length; a plant is refused too high as well as too low; a body standing still plants on its own footing. 20 and 40 cm ledges 2-15 cm away: feet 30-50 cm up -> on the floor.
- **Walked step-ups, not hops:** the clay's height table rises in a gravity curve before a 20-40 cm step and counted those frames as in the air (a mid-walk clay swap; with the clay retired, the body would hang up to 19 cm over the floor). The lifelike body now stands at `stage._walkHeightAt` (a step's rise over a quarter second, a ramp followed exactly, only rises or drops over 45 cm are jumps: `_walkAirborneAt`), which also counts the set's boxes like the feet do: keyed flat over set-box stairs, 75 cm into the landing and 21 cm/frame slides -> 0 ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs)). Parked-crate steps of 20 / 30 / 40 cm: 4-6 hidden frames -> 0.
- **Sprints:** 8-10 m/s had neither foot within 5 mm on 96-111 of 121 frames. A 1-frame stance is kept at a run's speed with a contact speed limit up to 45 % of the body's speed, the clip's own touch-down frames get no swing clearance, and a touch-down next to a plant is held: at most 2-4 frames in a row with no foot within 1 cm at 4-10 m/s (the flight phase), slides under 0.8 cm a frame. Browser, 8 m/s on the platform: 91-101 floating frames -> 39-41, lower foot 95th percentile 50-72 mm -> 33-44 mm (flight).
- **The swim gate is per frame** (`avatarcore.swimsAt`): a person who walks and then swims is grounded on the walk; `avatars.footPlan(stage, a)` builds a plan whether or not the body shows.
- **Cost:** the plan looked up the height table (JSON signature included) and the support shapes for every sole point of every frame; now once per frame: a key edit 36-57 -> ~20 ms for 5 s, 224-274 -> ~115 ms for 30 s, 0.2 ms a frame after.
- **Also:** 20 degree hill 5-15 floating frames -> 0-2, stairs up to 8 -> 0; the station ramp's slide limit in footplant.test back to 1.0 cm (the dock and the turn on the spot 1.2-1.3, from 1.5). Unchanged: browser flat walk, ramp walk, dock walk, idle turn (0 floating, planted 0 mm, slides 2-6 mm, before and after).
- **Tests:** [tests/js/footground.test.mjs](tests/js/footground.test.mjs): walk-stop-go and U-turns (both fail on 3abc644), standing off the station edge (x 1.55-1.61, both headings) and 20 / 40 cm ledges 2-15 cm back, parked-crate step-ups, flat-keyed set-box stairs, walk then swim, sprints and 0 -> 7 m/s (every stride touches down), the 20 degree hill and short rough ground; [tests/js/footkit.mjs](tests/js/footkit.mjs) `physical()` now counts every frame it leaves out (hidden, skipped) and `check()` fails on them; [tests/js/footplant.test.mjs](tests/js/footplant.test.mjs): key-edit cost and the drag deferral.

## 2026-11-07

**Feet, second pass: the station edge, swings over kerbs and stairs, nothing floating on uneven ground, runs and slow walks planted, fast spins step, walking backwards; the gate is "feet on the ground" (private branch `private/feet`, 23:24 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked in Node only on the real Rocketbox files over a headless stage, with measures of every shoe vertex against the true ground on every frame: not watched in the browser, the try-it demo or ComfyUI, no GPU render, so nobody has looked at it moving yet. The sun shadow that was the biggest part of the "floating" look is untouched. Stairs (five 15 cm steps, the body keyed up the pitch line) still have up to 8 frames at the step-ups with no foot within 5 mm of a tread, and a single-support hip dip of up to 5 cm (the reach guard's 6 cm allowance) instead of floating; a sole slides up to 1.04 cm in a frame there. A sprint (6 m/s) plants each foot only 2 frames a stride; runs have touching soles moving up to 1.6 cm a frame. A fast turn twists the leg: a planted foot turns half the body's turn, at most 0.07 rad a frame, and the hips still turn as a block. The heel lift while pivoting is a pitch of the whole foot (the toes don't bend for it). Walking backwards plays the walk clip in reverse (there is no backwards clip). A Pose key that bends a leg (a kneel) would be fought by the IK: Pose keys are now on the grounded side of the gate, and nothing checks what the pose is. Today a person who shows as clay is still not posed lifelike at all; the new gate only decides once the clay is retired (the one-motion plan). The plan now takes about 32 ms per person for a 5 s shot (was 15 ms), so a live drag that changes keys rebuilds it every tick. `web/stage/**` and `web/stage/sets/**` are the other lane's files in CLAUDE.md; the station ramp change in particular is set code, to be agreed at merge. The old footplant tests' slide limits were loosened from 1.0 to 1.5 cm on the ramp, the deck and the slow turn, because the measure changed (a touching vertex followed while it touches, over the whole shot, instead of one vertex of a mid-stance pose that no longer exists now that planted feet roll).
- **The station edge (a regression in the first pass):** the slope fit picked heel / toe / side by the foot bone's local Z, but on the Rocketbox skeleton forward is local +Y and up local -X, so all three samples were on the forefoot: a man or woman standing 4-10 cm in from the real platform edge facing the track sank 12.2-12.8 cm into the platform with the hips at the 12 cm cap. Now they are found along the rig's fwd / up; an edge or step under the sole leaves the foot level on the highest ground under it; each frame is lowered until the sole point nearest the ground under IT touches; a reach guard refuses planted targets the leg can't reach ([web/stage/people/footplant.mjs](web/stage/people/footplant.mjs) `planFeet`).
- **Swings into stairs, a kerb, bumps** (-17.7, -11.5, -4.2 cm): clearance used the ground under the ankle. Now every sole point, including the shoe's rim, toe cap and heel back (`footRig`), against the plan's own ground (`plan.ground`), and a moving foot that isn't planted clears it more the further it is from its plant, so a sole only touches where it plants. Stairs now plant (stance from the sole over the body's own footing, with the climb taken out of the contact speed).
- **Floating on uneven ground** (8 blend frames with neither foot down, up to 7.9 cm): between plants the foot now moves up or down by the ground, from one plant's offset to the next (`plan.dY`), and a foot the clip drags along the floor between two plants is a lifted step. Every walk at 0.3-1.2 m/s, the kerb, the bumps and the station ramp have a foot on the ground on every frame.
- **Runs at 3-6 m/s planted** (one foot had 0 frames at 3 m/s, none at 4-6): stance uses the contact speed (the slowest sole point near the lowest), widened with the body's speed, and 2-frame stances at a sprint; shorter blend ramps at speed.
- **Hip dips on slow walks** (7.5 cm at 0.5 m/s, the 12 cm cap at 0.3 m/s): the cause was the gait: in the idle-walk blend the stance foot crept forward because the walk's stride wasn't divided by its weight ([web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs](web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs) `gaitTable`). Plus the reach guard (2 cm, 6 cm when it's the only foot down) and planted frames that keep the clip's own heel-to-toe roll with the touching patch pinned. Walks now stay within 2 cm.
- **Fast spins** (a 360 in 1 s took 0 steps and slid 5.5 cm; in 2 s 5.8 cm a frame): stance is found with the body's turn taken out, steps are quicker in a fast turn (4 frames at least), and a planted foot turns on its ball with the heel lifted: 3 + 3 steps in 1 s, planted soles under 0.6 cm a frame. **Walking backwards** plants (the phase runs back when the body moves against its heading; it planted nothing).
- **Station ramp:** `stationFloor` returned 0 up the ramp and then 0.92 m at z = -70, and the drawn ramp slab was tilted the wrong way (0.92 m high 8 m out, at the ground by the platform). Both now run from the platform top at z0 down to the ground 8 m out (`STATION.rampLen`), within 0.7 mm of each other ([web/stage/sets/train.mjs](web/stage/sets/train.mjs)).
- **The gate:** grounding was skipped for any clay reason; now `feetUpReason` decides (off the feet: riding / seated, carried, physics, knocked over / tipped / lying, swimming / floating, in the air), so props, gaze, Pose keys, mocap, driven and Blocking people will be grounded once the clay is retired ([web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs) `avatarsAt`, `footPlanFor`).
- **Tests:** [tests/js/footground.test.mjs](tests/js/footground.test.mjs) (7) with the shared physical measures in [tests/js/footkit.mjs](tests/js/footkit.mjs): the real station edge for both people and facings, walks, runs, kerb, stairs, bumps, the station ramp (raycast against the drawn set), spins, walking backwards, the gate. On e56d6bc every one fails on the physics except the gate test (a new function). [tests/js/footplant.test.mjs](tests/js/footplant.test.mjs) (13) now uses the same fixtures and a physical slide measure. Full suites: JS 1658 tests, 1656 pass, 0 fail; Python 1807 passed, 25 skipped. Docs: [web/stage/people/README.md](web/stage/people/README.md) (Foot grounding), [web/stage/STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md) (`footOut.miss`, `feetUpReason`).

**Lifelike people stand on the ground: planted feet are locked, legs reach the floor with IK, platforms, ramps and decks, turns on the spot take steps (private branch `private/feet`, 22:31 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked in Node only, on the real Rocketbox files over a headless stage: not watched in the browser, the try-it demo or ComfyUI, and no GPU render, so nobody has looked at the result yet. The biggest part of the "floating" look in the measurement was the **sun shadow**, which this pass does not touch: 2048 px over a fixed +-25 m box (2.4 cm a texel), soft blur, no bias, so a sole on the floor still looks detached (a tighter box joined the shadow to the soles in the measurement's test render; a contact shadow is the other option). A planted foot is locked rigid at its mid-stance pose: the clip's heel-to-toe roll inside a stance is gone (the heel lifts only when the stance ends, over the 3-frame blend). At a contact change the sole moves up to 2.9 cm a frame for the man, 0.15 cm a frame more than the clip's own fastest swing. The turn steps are a lifted slide from one spot to the next (no knee lift of their own, no step pattern from a real turn), the hips still turn as a block over the planted feet (up to about 20 degrees of twist), and a fast spin can outpace one-foot-at-a-time stepping. The ramp, platform and deck were tested as a sloped set floor, a 0.92 m set floor and a 1.0 m set box with the person keyed on it, not the real station or lake sets (the measurement found the station's ramp is visual only: its walkable floor is flat). The plan is rebuilt whenever a key changes, so a live drag rebuilds it every tick (about 15 ms per person for a 5 s shot). The lane rules in CLAUDE.md give `web/stage/**` to the other agent; this was built on the private branch as the workflow asked, so who merges it is still to be agreed.
- **What Mike saw** ("even in the lifelike they are floating a little bit"): measured before any change (try-it demo, real GPU render, man and woman, 120 frames, 1.26 m/s): planted feet were within about 1 cm of the floor but slid 5-9 cm/s (3-5 cm a step), the idle's right foot hovered 3-5 mm, the idle-walk crossfade sank the feet up to 26 mm into the floor for 4-5 frames, and a turn on the spot swivelled the body over feet sliding 10-23 cm/s.
- **The static offset, at its source:** the body was lowered by the idle's first frame only; it now follows the playing clips' own floors (each clip's median standing sole height, blended by the clip weights), so the run's planted foot no longer stands 2 cm into the floor ([web/stage/people/footplant.mjs](web/stage/people/footplant.mjs) `clipFloors`, `floorFor`).
- **Foot grounding on the avatar's own skeleton** ([web/stage/people/footplant.mjs](web/stage/people/footplant.mjs), called by [web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs) `avatarsAt`): the sole vertices found once per avatar; one plan per person per shot (stance from sole height and speed with hysteresis, each stance locked at mid-stance, pitched to the ground's slope and lowered onto it, long drifting stances split into one-foot-at-a-time steps, 3-frame blends); per frame the pelvis comes down when out of reach, two-bone IK puts each foot on its target, a swinging foot is kept above the ground. It never reads the clay figure, so it carries over when one motion drives both the avatar and the logic rig. Frames in the air, a physics lift, and everyone who shows as clay are untouched.
- **Two bugs found on the way:** three's `PropertyMixer` writes a bone only when the clip's value changed, so posing the same frame again kept the last IK values (now put back before the mixer runs); and the raw pose read stale bone world matrices (now updated after posing). Both made the feet depend on the scrub order until fixed.
- **Tests** ([tests/js/footplant.test.mjs](tests/js/footplant.test.mjs), 13): man and woman, planted soles within 1 cm on flat ground, a platform, a ramp (sole pitched to it) and a deck; the mid-stance vertex moves 0.00 cm over each stance (limit 1 cm); no sole 1 cm into the floor through a walk-to-stop and a 2.4 m/s run; no pop at contact changes; the same feet in forward, scrambled and reverse order and with the plan rebuilt at any frame; turning on the spot steps each foot at least twice, never both at once; a hop's air frames and a physics lift untouched, a rider never planned; the pure parts (stance runs, step schedule, IK, floors). Full suites: JS 1651 tests, 1647 pass, 2 fail (`ask_robust` "root cause 4" and `oneclick_sweep` "stopped answering", the known flakes: both pass alone), 1 skipped, 1 todo; Python 1807 passed, 25 skipped. Docs: [web/stage/people/README.md](web/stage/people/README.md) (Foot grounding), [web/stage/STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md) (`stage.footGrounding`).

**Water, fourth pass: keyed dive-ins hold their depth again, scrubbing is order-free, a car drives into the tank, people placed or dragged onto a dock or deck stand on it, hops and swells keep them aboard (private branch `private/buoyancy`, 21:31 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** the browser check is a bare stage page (stage.mjs in Chrome through a local static server, real WebGL) read by numbers per frame, with two screenshots; placeInFrame on the lake used a test camera over the dock (the shot camera overridden, as in the Node test), not a camera set in the App's UI; not watched inside ComfyUI or the try-it demo; no GPU render. A car driven in from outside at the floor now starts at the tank bottom and floats up over ~1 s (the bottom of its box is 11 cm under its origin, so it steps up 11 cm on the entry frame). Support over the water now reaches 1.5 m above a dock or deck: a person keyed up to 1.5 m over a dock stands on it (on land a keyed height is kept); a swimmer keyed up to 0.9 m under a boat's deck is put on the deck. A hop's height is measured from the lowest key of that stretch on the support, so a key that slopes down along the dock reads as a hop. Leaving the tank through its wall still clamps (only the drive in was fixed). The settled can't-swim woman's bounding box dips 1.5 cm under the floor in the browser (the floor lift measures the visible clay only). The drag from outside the tank is stopped by the tank's 4 m wall in the live world (as before), so the browser drag was done inside the tank.
- **Keyed dive-ins (regression from the third pass):** flutter / frog / stroke keyed [0: 4.5 m], [24: 0.6 m], [144: 0.6 m] kicked up and swam at the surface from f48 (hips ~2.0; b5d87d0 held ~1.5 under). `personTrack` ([web/stage/buoyancy.mjs](web/stage/buoyancy.mjs)) now covers only the fall, the plunge (and the surface until the keys go under) and eases back to the keyed path: hips 1.54 at f36, 1.50-1.53 to f144 (browser too).
- **Scrubbing (regression):** `applySwim` ([web/stage/swim.mjs](web/stage/swim.mjs)) lifted the clay off the floor before it bent the fins, routed the hose and floated the hair, so the lift saw the frame shown before: the dive-in's f29 was 1.5523 or 1.5589 by order, and a can't-swim woman settled on the bottom jumped up to 5 cm when scrubbed back (181 of 900 frames). The lift runs last now: 0 mismatches in 435 out-of-order frames (browser too).
- **A car driven into the tank** (x -14 to 0 over 48 frames) jumped 2.7 m at f22 and stalled at -5.39 to f28: `_buoyTrack` keeps a drive-in on its keys while its footprint crosses the wall; `floatTrack` lets something coming in from outside go on from its keyed height. Steps 0.16-0.36 m per frame, never over 0.15 m vertically.
- **Placed or dragged onto a dock or deck:** placeInFrame and a live drag key a person at 0, and support was only looked for above 0.05 m (a man placed on the dock stood on the lake bed, head through it). Keyed at 0 over the water, support is looked for from 3 m above the surface and the body stands at it. A swimmer dragged onto the speedboat is seated aboard (the drag mounts them) instead of being left in the swim pose inside the hull.
- **Hops, high keys, swells:** a 0.5 m hop on the dock went 0.78 m through it, a man keyed 0.2 m over it fell to the lake floor, a man on the speedboat's deck on a swell went into the sea (230 of 241 frames keyed at the f0 deck height). Now they stand at the support plus the keys' rise over their lowest key there: the hop arcs 0.5 m and lands back, the high key stands on the dock, the deck's ~1 m heave carries them (feet within 5 cm of it, 0 frames in the sea).
- **The intermittent JS failure** `tryit_scriptload` 'Run asks the shell for an example first, then replays it': the file and api.js are unchanged from `main`; the test waited a fixed 900 ms for a replay whose rungs end ~730 ms after Run. With a 250 ms stall injected after Run it fails on `main` every time; it now waits for the replay's `execution_success` (10 s cap) and passes with the stall.
- **Tests** ([tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs](tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs), 26): new: the keyed dive-in (and keyed under only at the end), the out-of-order scrub (dive-in in four modes, can't swim / sink slowly settling), the drive-in car, placed / dragged onto the dock and the boat, the hop / high key / swell; each fails on 8fc04ef. [docs/swim.md](docs/swim.md) updated.

**Water, third pass: people stand on the dock and on a boat's deck, jumps can be timed, every Swim mode drops in, nothing floats through the tank wall (private branch `private/buoyancy`, 20:56 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked by Node tests on a headless stage only: not watched in the browser, the try-it demo or ComfyUI, and no GPU render. A person on a boat's deck stands at their keyed height: they don't ride the boat's heave, pitch or roll (a mount does; in the glassy tank the deck moves under a centimetre, on a swell they would float off it or sink into it). Walking on the deck, the planted feet use one height for the whole body (the deck under the person, from `_deckAt`), so a step onto a bench seat isn't climbed. The deck is found by ray-casting the boat's clay meshes posed at each frame (cached in the swim plan); the dock and other set boxes need the set's group (a headless stage that drops it sees no dock). A held jump follows only keys that hold or rise from frame 0: a run that starts later (walking off the edge) drops at once, even if its keys hold mid-air. The edge clamp moves the render, not the key, and a boat's own float track still runs on the unclamped path. Mike's exact tank scene isn't known: it was reproduced as the closest cases below.
- **Mike's report reproduced first** ("when I placed a person in a water tank, they just went to the bottom and started walking"): on `main`, a man keyed 4.5 m over the tank with a hold and a step walked on the tank floor (hips 0.81, the walk cycle on every frame), and a man on the lake's dock fell through to y 0.17 and walked there. On this branch before this pass, the dock man still fell in at f0 and waded on the bottom (hips 0.96), held keys at 4.5 m fell at f0, and in mid-air the gait put the held man's feet on the tank floor (hips 0.93).
- **Dock and deck:** `personTrack` takes `support[f]` (stage `_standOnAt`: set boxes, parked props not in the water, and a boat's deck at that frame by `_deckAt`); keyed on it (-5..+15 cm) the person is off the water, on the gait, and the gait's feet stand at that height (`_standOnKeys`). A ledge under water counts only if they could stand there with the head out.
- **Timed jumps:** the fall starts at the last frame before the keys start going down (`held[f]` until then), or the first frame off a support; keys that never go down still drop at f0 (the earlier tests unchanged).
- **Flutter, frog, dive** keyed above the surface now drop in (was: treading / hovering from f0, against docs/swim.md); keyed in the water they keep their keyed depth.
- **Tank edge:** `_insideWater` (Add object's footprint fit, now a pure helper) runs on keyed and dragged positions in `_waterPhysics` and the live drag; a tipped object near the wall is measured as posed and moved back in. A car keyed at x 7.5 side-on reached x 9.83 (wall at 7.8); now 7.75-7.80.
- **Tests** ([tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs](tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs), 21): new: dock and deck (standing and walking, and stepping off the dock's end), the timed jump (with personTrack's held frames and a scrub check), flutter / frog / dive from above, the car at the wall (keyed and dragged); all four fail on b5d87d0. The standing-depth test gets a man walking through the 1.2 m lake (swimming off, walking on), which fails on 86ef579 (it passed there before). Full suites: JS 1633 tests, 1631 pass, 0 fail (1 skipped, 1 todo) on the second run; in the first full run "a reshoot whose server stopped answering times out" (oneclick_sweep.test.mjs: a 25 ms timeout racing the first poll) failed and passed alone 3 of 3 and in the next full run (that first run also caught a crash of this pass in people_m16's bare stage with no scene root, fixed before the commits); nothing in web/oneclick changed on this branch. Python 1807 passed, 25 skipped.

**Water tank, second pass: a person dropped in never stands on the floor; can't swim fits a short shot; animals sink; wading; light floats stay up (private branch `private/buoyancy`, 20:17 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** the browser check is a bare stage page (web/stage/stage.mjs in Chrome through a local static server, real WebGL) read by numbers per frame; the framing of its screenshots was off, so nothing was judged by eye, not inside ComfyUI or the try-it demo, and no GPU render. The plunge is drawn, not integrated: an even deceleration over 0.35 m + 0.75 x the drop height (capped 30 cm above the floor), a kick up at 0.8 m/s (a non-swimmer flails up at 0.4 m/s); the depth figure is a recalled rule of thumb for a feet-first entry, not measured. Wading is decided by one number (depth <= 0.86 x height) for Auto and Can't swim only; Tread / strokes / Back / Sink slowly still swim in shallows (the new floor clamp keeps their feet out of the floor, so treading in 1.2 m pushes the body up rather than kneeling). After a drop into shallows they stand in a fixed "wade" pose and do not walk even when keyed moving. A sinking animal keyed to travel still slides along the bottom while lying on its side. The entry slam factor (2.5x the added mass for light floats) is a film choice tuned to the ball and crate below, not a measured slamming coefficient; the ball still bobs many small times (its damping ratio 0.06 is unchanged). The floor clamp scans the clay's vertices each frame a swimmer is within a body height of the floor (cost not profiled on big crowds).

Now (headless and in the browser): lowest point 0.50 / 0.58 m, under water only in "fall", "kickup" (new swim pose: arms sweeping down, whip kick) or "struggle"; the man treads from f48, the woman struggles to f102 and is sinking limp by f120 (her clay's bottom 0.70 at f141). ([web/stage/buoyancy.mjs](web/stage/buoyancy.mjs) `plungeTrack`, `personTrack`; [web/stage/swim.mjs](web/stage/swim.mjs) `kickup`, `wade`)
- **Can't swim in a short shot:** the 2-3.5 s struggle now starts at water entry (was: from reaching the surface, so a dropped-in woman was still at the surface at f141); the sink after a struggle starts at 1010 kg/m3 (the lungs emptied) and goes to 1045 over 1.5 s: ~0.24 m/s.
- **Animals set to Can't swim / Sink slowly** were on the tank floor from frame 0 with the walk cycle running (dog y -0.05 every frame): now they float at their own density, paddle hard head-up for the seeded struggle (none for Sink slowly), sink nose-down (density to 1025: ~0.4 m/s) and lie on their side on the bottom, legs still (stage `_animalSinkPose`).
- **No legs through the floor:** a sinking person was lowered in the upright limp pose (legs 0.39 m under, in Depth and Pose too); the hips now go down with the tip-over onto the back, and `applySwim` keeps the posed clay's lowest vertex on the pool floor.
- **Standing depth:** Can't swim drowned a 1.8 m man in the 1.2 m lake and the 1.5 m open water (body at -0.47); `canStand` (the kind's real height) makes Auto / Can't swim stand and wade there on the gait; a 1.68 m woman still can't stand in 1.5 m.
- **Info that matches:** `buoyancyInfo` said 'floats, 99% under' for a Can't-swim person and `setDensity(person, 'metal')` said ok / 'sinks 13 m/s' with no effect: people on a swim plan now get null / a refusal and a new `swimInfo` (also `physicsInfo().swim`, shown by the Physics panel instead of Floats / sinks); a sinking animal reports where it ends up.
- **Light floats on entry:** momentum shared with the growing added mass (slam 2.5x for light floats, 1x above ~1200 kg/m3): from 2 m a ball's bottom goes 0.23 m under (was 1.1 m, fully under at f24), a crate 0.62 m with its top out (was fully under); a rock still reaches the floor by 1 s.
- **Add object** fits the whole footprint inside the tank's inner wall (a car reached z -9.33, a speedboat -8.29, wall at +-7.8).
- **Tests** ([tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs](tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs), 17): new: the browser scenario, the short-shot struggle, the sinking floor clamp, wading, animals, the info, ball / crate entry, large Add object; tests 3 and 4 tightened (waterlines against weight, rock down by 1 s, keyed-under starts where keyed): checked to fail on cb57dad, and every new test fails on d1c1302. [tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs](tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs): its can't-swim pool is 2.5 m deep (a 1.8 m man wades in 1.2 m now, lifelike body shown). **Buoyancy in the Water tank: things float or sink by weight, people swim (or can't), nobody walks on the bottom (private branch `private/buoyancy`, 19:40 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests on a headless stage only: not watched in the browser, the try-it demo (not rebuilt: its build writes cache files outside the worktree) or ComfyUI, and no GPU render. A swimmer of any mode shows as **clay** for the whole shot (the documented fallback, reason "swims"): the lifelike body has no swim, struggle or sink clips. Film compression: a non-swimmer struggles 2-3.5 s (real: 20-60 s), wood and cardboard soak and a car floods in tens of seconds (real: hours; 30-120 s for a car). Not modelled: lungs compressing with depth (Boyle), clothes ballooning, a weighted bottom walk, the floor of a set other than flat 0, waves under a floating prop in open water (it floats on the flat level), a prop thrown into the tank from outside after a bake (its bake runs on the floor; the water takes over when its baked path is over the water, from the baked height and speed there). `swim: "off"` changed meaning: it used to float a person upright like a cork, it is **Can't swim** now (two avatar tests updated for it). The densities and speeds are from the research notes (recalled sources, not re-checked). On the lane rules: `web/stage/**` is the other agent's lane; this was assigned directly on a private branch.
- **What Mike saw, reproduced first:** a man in the tank dragged live fell from treading (y 1.098) to the tank floor in ~15 frames and walked there: the live world ([web/stage/live.mjs](web/stage/live.mjs)) has a floor and no water, set his height from it and added the walk on top of the swim. Now a person over the water keeps swimming while dragged (only x / z follow; the key keeps its height, 0 = placed in the water), props in the water are static in the live world, a prop carried over the tank shows at its waterline and is keyed where it is let go (above the surface = a drop height), and `settle()` leaves a prop over the water to the water.
- **The other paths that skipped the water ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs)):** Simulate & bake leaves dynamic objects let go over a pool to the water (they fell to the tank floor at full gravity and broke there; the message says how many); the water now runs after the bake and the Physics start height, which are a drop height (a ball with Height 2 floated 2 m over the water); the water measures the clay's own local box (the posed group's world box included a hidden lifelike body and sank a person further every time the same frame was set: -0.05, -6.18, -11.38 m); a person keyed above the surface drops in, goes under and comes up (was: clay through the floor, the lifelike body standing in mid-air); Add object on the Water tank set keeps new things inside the walls.
- **Weight-based buoyancy ([web/stage/buoyancy.mjs](web/stage/buoyancy.mjs), new):** replaces the kind lists (SINK, a submerged-fraction table, fixed sink speeds). Every object has a density (kind / material table, or its Physics mass over its volume, or the new per-object **Floats / sinks** density) and a volume from its bounds; (ρ_w V_sub − m) g with quadratic drag, added mass and surface damping, integrated once per object per shot on 12 sub-steps a frame and cached on its keys (like `_floatsInShot`), so every frame is a lookup. A float plunges, bobs under-damped and settles at its real waterline (a ball higher than a crate); a rock sinks at ~4 m/s and settles; a crate soaks and sits lower but floats; a cardboard box soaks through and sinks; a car floats low and tips nose down as it floods. Keyed under the surface it rises or sinks from there (no snap). Boats keep their hull physics.
- **Swim control per person** (person panel **Swim**, `stage.setSwim(id, mode)` for Ask): Tread water (head up), the strokes (swim.mjs styles unchanged), Float on back, Dive / scuba (neutral, hovers), the underwater kicks, **Can't swim / not treading** (the instinctive drowning response at the surface, then a slow limp sink, ~0.26 m/s terminal, and a settle on the bottom on their back) and **Sink slowly** (unconscious). New swim.mjs track styles (struggle, limp, limp_floor, backfloat, fall) cross-fade over ±6 frames; plans without them are unchanged.
- **Physics panel ([web/stage/physpanel.mjs](web/stage/physpanel.mjs)):** **Floats / sinks** (Auto, foam / wood / low / neutral / slow sink / stone / steel) with a line giving its density, where it comes from and whether it floats (how deep) or sinks (how fast). Stage API: `setDensity`, `buoyancyInfo`, `setSwim` ([web/stage/STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md)); [docs/swim.md](docs/swim.md) has the tables; tutorial step 5 under Swimming; knowledge pack regenerated.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs](tests/js/buoyancy.test.mjs) (12): the live-drag repro, the carried crate and settle(), rock faster than a can't-swim person (0.1-0.3 m/s) slower than a soaking crate floats, a ball higher than a crate, the under-damped bob to the waterline, keyed-under rise / sink, mass and density overrides flip the outcome, scrubbing in any order and re-setting a frame are exact, tread keeps the head out, back float, can't-swim struggle / sink / bottom with no walking, a drop-in person, the bake and the start height, Add object, the Swim select. JS: 1624 tests, 1621 passed, 1 failed (the known `oneclick_sweep` flake: 5 / 5 pass alone), 1 skipped, 1 todo (the known try-it re-publish). Python: 1807 passed, 25 skipped.

**Third review fixes: spinning or rising props, orbit speed at keys, shaders compiled in a real browser (private branch `private/stage-next`, 18:55 by this machine's clock)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** a prop whose keys turn it (heading / roll / pitch over 1 degree) or raise it (key_y, over 5 cm) is now treated like a travelling prop, so people no longer step up onto it at all: a turntable crate or a lift platform is walked through, not climbed (before, it was climbed but rebuilt everything every frame). The orbit fix lengthens a tangent from chord to arc (theta / sin theta), which is exact only for keys evenly spaced in time and along a circle; uneven spacing round a bend is approximate, and turns between 60 and 90 degrees blend the old per-axis limiter in. Moving shots saved on curve 2 play slightly differently through curved interior keys (straight runs are unchanged). The shader check runs in a software WebGL (SwiftShader in Playwright's cached headless Chromium on this machine), not on a GPU or inside ComfyUI, and only the depth pass and the selection outline are covered (not the water, cards or fracture shaders). Nothing here was checked in the App itself.
- **Spinning / rising props (`web/stage/stage.mjs` `keyedMoving`, used by `_obstacles`):** the parked test looked only at x / z travel, so a crate keyed to spin 360 degrees in 48 frames passed OBS_JUMP every frame, re-anchored its grid and rebuilt its box and every person's whole-shot height table on all 49 frames; one rising 40 cm crossed 9 grid cells (9 rebuilds). Now 0 rebuilds for both (test `tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs`, failed 49 and 9 before).
- **Orbit speed at keys (`web/stage/camcurve.mjs` `tangents`):** the per-axis Fritsch-Carlson limiter zeroed an axis whose neighbouring keys share a value (an orbit's z at 72 and 108 degrees), so the camera slowed at those keys: 0.117 vs 0.142 m/frame (1.22x) on a 5 m orbit keyed every 36 degrees. The limit now follows the path's turn: up to 60 degrees between the chords the tangent is limited as a vector (3x the slower side) and lengthened from chord to arc; past 90 degrees it is per axis as before (out and back, sharp corners). Orbit speed max / min across interior keys is now within 1.05; out-and-back and a 90-degree corner still never swing past their key (test `tests/js/camcurve.test.mjs`).
- **Depth and outline shaders compiled (`tests/browser/shaders.html`, `tests/browser/run_shaders.mjs`, `tests/js/shaders_browser.test.mjs`):** neither had ever been compiled by a test. A headless Chromium already on the machine (nothing installed) renders a bent two-bone skinned mesh, a plain mesh and a cut-out card: the Depth silhouette matches the colour one (overlap 1.0 each; the bind pose overlaps the posed mesh only 0.41, so a Depth pass that ignored skinning fails), and the selection outline draws a ring hugging the posed mesh with nothing over it. Removing the skinning chunk, breaking the GLSL or breaking the stencil mask each fails the test. With no browser found the test is skipped and says so; `node tests/browser/run_shaders.mjs` (or `--browser /path`, or `SOUNDSTAGE_BROWSER`) runs it by hand.

**Browser check of the second review fixes (c93ce9b): old shots, the key jump, speed at keys, people (private branch `private/stage-next`, 18:24 by this machine's clock)**

Even speed ignores interior key times by design, so the camera misses the interior keys (by up to 3.6 m in the test shot) and its ends ease from and to rest (frame-to-frame ratio up to 1.60 there). **Second review fixes: old-shot key jump, even speed on a pan, obstacle order dependence (private branch `private/stage-next`, 18:15 by this machine's clock, which is behind the times on some entries below)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests only, not in the browser or the App. Three defects the second review confirmed were shipped in the entries below and are fixed here: (1) on an old shot (no `cam_curve`), adding a key, the first orbit drag or the first wheel zoom at an unkeyed frame made the camera jump, because `_editCamera` switched the shot to curve 2 before reading the camera for the new key (C3's keys at frame 30: shown z -7.325 / 38.50 mm, written z -7.475 / 37.95 mm); (2) even speed timed the move by the camera position's path only and used the target only when the camera stood perfectly still, so a pan with a small dolly played unevenly (target speed max / min 4.8); (3) the obstacle hysteresis counter (5419934) kept whatever transform it last counted, so feet over a prop that settles or jitters could differ by up to about 5 cm between sequential play and a jump to the frame, which broke C3's order-independence gate (the new test fails on that code at frame 12). Limits that remain: the obstacle boxes are now a pure function of the prop's transform only once the prop's grid is anchored. The anchor is where the stage first sees the group (its build) or where an edit last put it (a step of more than 25 cm, about 6 degrees or 10 % between two looks). So a rebuild at another frame, or a seek across a fall bigger than that, re-anchors and can shift the boxes by up to half a cell (2.5 cm); a prop moved off its anchor sits on the grid, up to 2.5 cm (about half a degree, 0.5 %) from its live transform; something floating in water still uses its live transform at the last rebuild (its bob is left out, as before). Even speed now evens the camera and target travel combined; when both move a lot at different rates, each alone can still vary. Focus and iris keys (`lens_keys`) stay on frame time under even speed, so a focus pull can drift off the move it was keyed to. The phone viewfinder's take turns even speed off for the shot without telling the user (`viewfinder.mjs` `applyKeys` deletes `cam_speed`; the desktop take says so in its status line, the phone doesn't), and so does a rig snap (`setCameraKeys(..., {camSpeed: null})`, status "Snapped to ..." only). `setCameraKeys` still treats a new key set equal to an old shot's keys as a copy and keeps the old curve.
- **Fixed** ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/stage/camcurve.mjs](web/stage/camcurve.mjs)): (1) `_editCamera` reads the camera under the curve the shot plays now, writes the key, then switches the shot to curve 2: the key, the orbit drag's start and the zoom's start are what was on screen. (2) Even speed's arc-length table measures `hypot(camera travel, target travel)`: a dolly, a pan, a pan with a small dolly and a tracking move each come out at one speed. (3) `_obstacles` puts each parked prop's transform on a grid (5 cm, 0.01 per quaternion component, 1 %) anchored as above and builds the box from the cell's own transform, not the live one; a prop at its anchor (one that doesn't move) is exact. The last 8 obstacle lists and the last 4 height tables per person stay cached, so jitter across a cell edge flips between cached ones instead of rebuilding a box or a height table every frame. Docs: [docs/vcam.md](docs/vcam.md), [web/stage/STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md); knowledge pack regenerated.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/camcurve.test.mjs](tests/js/camcurve.test.mjs): on an old shot at frame 30, Set key here, the first orbit drag (`_activeOrbit`), a zero-pixel orbit drag (`_navDrag`, held to 1 mm because a drag writes millimetres) and a lens-key edit each keep the shown position, target and focal in the key and on screen (fails before: z -7.825 / 37.947 mm written). Even speed on the reviewer's pan with a small dolly: combined and target speed max / min within 1.05 mid-shot (8.60 combined before), also a large dolly with a differently timed pan. [tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs](tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs): feet over a settling, jittering prop (15 cm drop, decaying 8 mm jitter and wobble) are identical after sequential, shuffled and scrubbed evaluation; a 1 m fall seen out of order stays within 2.6 cm (the stated limit). The boundary-jitter test now counts boxes and tables built (at most 2 boxes and 3 cached lists over 48 frames; an 8 cm creep 1 to 3 rebuilds) instead of list identity. JS (`node --test tests/js/*.test.mjs`): 1609 tests, 1607 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped, 1 todo (the known try-it re-publish). Python (`python3 -m pytest tests -q`): 1807 passed, 25 skipped.

**Review fixes on the camera curve, the Book's forgetDecision and the obstacle signature (private branch `private/stage-next`, 19:20)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests only; none of this was watched in the browser or the App. Shots with no `cam_curve` still play the old curve: an old project's shots, a shot the scene agent's chat edit re-keys (`update_shot`), and keys deleted from the timeline rows or the camera path (`tracks.mjs`, `camviz.mjs`, `objparts.mjs` write keys directly and don't mark the curve). Spotting new shots from the agent works by shot id: a chat edit that makes a shot under an id already on the stage leaves it on the old curve. The agent's undo / redo of a build keeps the curve (the marked copy is the one replayed). The first time a new move goes through `setCameraKeys` the shot switches to the new curve whole, as a re-key already did. A take on an even-speed shot now turns even speed off for that shot, with no prompt (the desktop take says so in its status line; the phone viewfinder doesn't). The phone viewfinder and desktop take paths are checked through the stage and the fitter plus a source check of their calls, not by running `attachViewfinder` or the phone page. The obstacle hysteresis keeps the transform the tables were last built from, so a prop that creeps a few cm and stops keeps tables up to 5 cm (about 1 degree, 1 %) stale; the old rounding was off by up to 2.5 cm. `book.forgetDecision` is async now: anything calling it has to await it (Ask doesn't call it yet). **Deferred, not done:** the weak-test items of the people review (round 3 item 10): the Depth shader and the new card-cut outline shader are never compiled, cut-out passes are checked by material not by pixels, and the people tests build stand-in stages (`Object.create(Stage.prototype)` with the real methods) instead of a real `new Stage`. All three need WebGL, which Node doesn't have; they need a browser test run (the try-it page or ComfyUI) to close, and the reviewer has to accept the deferral.
- **Fixed** ([web/movefit/stabilize.mjs](web/movefit/stabilize.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/stage/camcurve.mjs](web/stage/camcurve.mjs), [web/hosthooks.mjs](web/hosthooks.mjs), [web/soundstage.js](web/soundstage.js), [web/vcam/desk.mjs](web/vcam/desk.mjs), [web/vcam/viewfinder.mjs](web/vcam/viewfinder.mjs), [web/book/book.mjs](web/book/book.mjs)): (1) `stageCameraAt(keys, frame, curve, speed)` plays `cam_speed: "even"` as the stage does. A phone or VCam take is fitted for curve 2 and put on with `setCameraKeys(keys, {curve: 2, camSpeed: null})`, so it plays at its own timing (even speed re-times by arc length whatever the key times are, so no key set can keep a take's timing under it). Discard puts the keys, curve and speed back. (2) More of the App's moves get the smooth curve. `stage.setCameraKeys` puts any new move on curve 2: presets, Advisor setups, takes, rig snaps and Cut setups framed from a preset. Only a copy of a shot still on the old curve keeps it (the Cut's copy and duplicate setup), so the copy moves like its source; `{curve}` sets it outright. A new node's Master and a fresh scene's Master are on curve 2, and so are the scene agent's new shots (`smoothAgentShots`: every shot of a build, and shots an edit adds, including the agent's redo copies). The `camcurve.mjs` header now says which shots get version 2. (3) The camera at an interior key is exactly that key (focal 85 reads 85, not 85.00000000000001): the key search returns the key's own segment. (4) `book.forgetDecision(id)` waits for the server POST and resolves `{ok: true}` only after it answers. On a failed save (HTTP error, `{error}`, or a network failure) it resolves `{ok: false, error}` and puts the note back where it was, so the book and the server agree. (5) The `_obstacles` signature uses hysteresis instead of fixed-step rounding. Each parked prop keeps the transform the tables were built from, and a counter moves on only past 5 cm, about 1 degree or 1 % (q and -q count as the same rotation). Jitter on a step boundary no longer rebuilds every frame: the reviewer's probe (x = 0.025 m, ±1 mm) rebuilt on 48 of 48 frames and now on 0. The M1.6 entry's claim is corrected below.
- **Review of e717768 (the `_peekOrbit` commit), at this branch's HEAD:** the remaining `_activeOrbit` callers are moves that key the shot on purpose (snapView, WASD and the held-key fly, the wheel zoom, drags, `_flyTo`, `frameSelected`). `_frameView` only runs from the double-click focus, which works in the Free view only. One behaviour change: in a follow-the-actor shot, `_peekOrbit` returns the world-space camera, while `_activeOrbit` returns the key in the actor's frame, so the drag pivot's fallback is now taken from the world camera. That is more correct for a world-space ray, but nothing tests it. On a frame with a key and a handheld or jib rig, it also includes the rig's offset. Both suites pass at this branch's HEAD (see Tests); the 4 navigation_stagehooks failures came from the then-uncommitted tree and pass here.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/camcurve.test.mjs](tests/js/camcurve.test.mjs): `setCameraKeys` (a new move goes to curve 2, a copy stays on curve 1, explicit curve and speed); `stageCameraAt` even-speed parity with the stage; a take with uneven timing on an even-speed shot plays on time (worst under 5 cm, inside the fitter's slip) where even speed would put it more than 0.5 m off; `smoothAgentShots` on a stage built from the real methods; exact focal and position at every key (failed before: 44.99999999999999 at key 48); the parity test now covers even speed and the rig. [tests/js/hosthooks.test.mjs](tests/js/hosthooks.test.mjs): `forgetDecision` stays pending until the POST answers, and a 500 or a rejected fetch gives `{ok: false}` with the note back in its old place; the wiring check covers `smoothAgentShots`. [tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs](tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs): boundary jitter on position, scale and rotation gives 0 rebuilds in 48 frames (48 before), an 8 cm creep 1 rebuild, a sign-flipped quaternion none. JS (`node --test tests/js/*.test.mjs`), after the last code commit: 1606 tests, 1603 passed, 1 failed, 1 skipped, 1 todo. The failure is the known `oneclick_sweep` "stopped answering" flake; it ran while the Python suite was also running, and passed alone (5/5). The todo is the known try-it re-publish. Python (`python3 -m pytest tests -q`): 1807 passed, 25 skipped. The knowledge pack is regenerated, and its freshness test passes.
