**Smooth camera curve: no speed jumps at camera keys (curve version 2; private branch `private/stage-next`, 17:13)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests only; nobody has watched a shot on the new curve in the browser or the App, or rendered one. Old shots keep the old curve until their camera is re-keyed on the stage (any key added, moved by orbiting the shot camera, or deleted at the playhead): that first re-key changes how the whole shot moves, not just near the edited key, and there is no on-screen control for the curve yet (only `stage.setCameraCurve`). Moving a key in the timeline strip (web/stage/tracks.mjs drag) does not switch a shot over. Shots made by the scene agent, presets, the advisor or the Cut's duplicate keep the version of what they come from, so in an old project most shots stay on the old curve. The phone / virtual-camera key fitter now fits a smooth-curve shot with that curve: on a synthetic S move it needed 29 keys where the old curve needed 19 (the zero end tangents ease the start and end, and the take starts already moving). Even speed ignores interior key times and holds by design. The preview still steps at shot fps and there is still no 3D motion blur (C3 items 5, 6 and 11 are not part of this). Actor paths keep the old curve.
- **What** ([web/stage/camcurve.mjs](web/stage/camcurve.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/coach/rules.mjs](web/coach/rules.mjs), [web/movefit/stabilize.mjs](web/movefit/stabilize.mjs), [web/vcam/live.mjs](web/vcam/live.mjs), [web/vcam/vfcore.mjs](web/vcam/vfcore.mjs), [web/vcam/desk.mjs](web/vcam/desk.mjs), [web/vcam/viewfinder.mjs](web/vcam/viewfinder.mjs), [web/soundstage.js](web/soundstage.js)): a shot with `cam_curve: 2` plays its camera keys as a cubic Hermite curve with tangents from the key times (the old uniform Catmull-Rom ignored them, the main cause of the bumps at uneven keys), a Fritsch-Carlson overshoot limiter, zero-velocity tangents at hold boundaries, easing only at the first and last keys (the old curve halved the speed at the first and last interior keys), and the focal length interpolated in log space (the old smoothstep stopped the zoom at every key). `cam_speed: "even"` re-times the path by arc length to one speed between short ease-in and ease-out. `stage.setCameraCurve("smooth" | "even" | "legacy")` and `stage.cameraCurve()`. The Coach's camera and track, the key fitter (`reduceKeys({curve})`, fed the shot's `cam_curve` by the App, the phone viewfinder and the desk) and the stage play the same curve; the rig cache keys on the version. Docs: [docs/vcam.md](docs/vcam.md), [docs/coach.md](docs/coach.md), [web/stage/STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md); knowledge pack regenerated.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/camcurve.test.mjs](tests/js/camcurve.test.mjs), C3's checks on keys at frames 0, 12, 48, 60, 96 (97 frames): the one-sided velocity jump at each interior key is at most 1.5x its neighbours' (the old curve fails this), keys are hit within 0.001, a hold stops truly still and never bows back, the log-focal rate at a key is at least 80% of its neighbours' (the old curve fails), even speed's mid-shot max / min speed is within 1.05 with 8,192 arc-length divisions (and for a pan-only camera), and frame N is identical after sequential, shuffled and scrubbed evaluation, with and without dolly and handheld rigs. Also: old shots play the old curve exactly and keep it through `setCameraKeys` and new setups; a key at the playhead moves a shot over; parity with the Coach (camera and full track, incl. a jib rig) and the fitter's playback; actors and takefix's replay unchanged; the fitter's keys for curve 2 play back closer on curve 2 than keys fitted for the old curve. Screen speed of a fixed grid at 1920x1080 on the C3 keys: old curve peak 287.2 px/frame with a worst frame-to-frame change of 121.6 px; new curve peak 251.1 px/frame, worst change 18.9 px.

**Ask hooks: `host.cutSig`, `host.quality` and `book.forgetDecision(id)` (private branch `private/stage-next`, 17:03)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests only, not in the App: the Cut test drives the real `CutPanel` edit and undo methods on a bare panel (no timeline drawing), the quality test runs the real `LtxAdvanced` and `Pipeline` methods on a tiny fake DOM, and the Book test mounts the real `mountBook` on that fake DOM. Ask's side (Jon's Claude, `web/agent/**`) is not wired to `cutSig` or `forgetDecision` yet; nothing here calls them. `cutSig` covers the Cut's undoable state (clips, audio, titles, subtitles, fades, graphics), not playhead or zoom. `quality.clear(["preset"])` puts the shot back on Final (the default) and keeps its seed, Adherence and LoRAs, as the panel's own preset buttons do. `forgetDecision` refuses notes the director wrote (only `source: "chat"` decisions go).
- **What** ([web/hosthooks.mjs](web/hosthooks.mjs), [web/soundstage.js](web/soundstage.js), [web/book/book.mjs](web/book/book.mjs)): (a) `mountDirector`'s host gets `cutSig`, a function returning the Cut's current signature (a hash of the state the Cut's Undo snapshots), so Ask can undo a transition it added only while it is still the last edit. (b) The Director's Book has `forgetDecision(id)`: removes that decision note, saves the workflow copy and POSTs `/soundstage/book/save` at once (no 1.2 s debounce), refreshes the views, and returns `{ok: true}` or `{ok: false, error}`. (c) `mountDirector`'s host gets `quality = {get(), set(values), clear(keys)}` for ⑤ Motion's Adherence, Layout IC-LoRA strength, Follow the clay and Draft / Final: `set` and `clear` call the same per-shot store, preset buttons and slider handler the Motion panel uses and refresh it. Ask's host now uses the same object instead of its own copy (preset is applied before Adherence; the result is the same). Documented in [docs/agent.md](docs/agent.md) (Integrator wiring 4); knowledge pack regenerated.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/hosthooks.test.mjs](tests/js/hosthooks.test.mjs): the wiring in `soundstage.js` (both hosts, one quality object); the signature on a real `CutPanel` transition, its Undo and a tail fade; `get` / `set` / `clear` on a real `LtxAdvanced` + `Pipeline._setAdherence` (saved per shot, the panel reading the same, the slider handler run, defaults back); `forgetDecision` on a mounted Book (note gone, saved to the server once and at once, no late save, unknown id and the director's own note refused).

**Lifelike people M1.6: the findings left open after review round 3 (private branch `private/stage-next`, 16:56)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests only; not seen in the browser or the App, and the new outline shader variant (hair / lash cut-outs in the selection outline) was never compiled (no GL in Node). A prop that is not placed by `placeInFrame` still sits at y = 0 on a raised set floor (buried in a station platform) unless it has `key_y`: only `placeInFrame` keys it at the floor now, older scenes and `addObject` are unchanged. The obstacle signature is quantised (5 cm, about 1 degree, 1 %), so a crate nudged by under 2.5 cm doesn't refresh the height tables until it moves further, and (found by review, corrected in the later "Review fixes" entry) jitter that sits on a rounding-step boundary still rebuilt them every frame (48 of 48 frames at x = 0.025 m with ±1 mm jitter); a crate floating in a pool uses the box from whatever bob phase it was built at, so stand-on heights on a floating prop can differ by a few cm between a sequential play and a jump to the frame. A swimming-off person who crosses the pool anywhere in the shot is now clay for the whole shot ("floats in the water"), on dry frames too. A failed avatar load is now remembered for 15 s, so a fix to the install shows only at a build after that. The weak-test items of the review (Depth shader never compiled, cut-out passes checked by material not pixels, stub stages instead of the real build path) are not addressed.
- **Fixed** ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs), [web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs](web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs), [web/stage/outline.mjs](web/stage/outline.mjs)): (1) `placeInFrame` no longer assumes the ground gets farther up the frame: it samples the whole screen column, bisects every crossing and keeps the one nearest the frame's centre, so a top-down camera 5 m up places 0.5, 1 and 2 m (it refused 1 m); too near / too far errors give the reach. (2) The `_obstacles` signature is coarse and quantised, and leaves out the live transform of anything floating (`userData.inWater`): bobbing pool props and boats on the swell no longer rebuild every person's height table each frame, and settling or jittering props don't either, except (corrected later the same day, see "Review fixes") jitter straddling a rounding step, which still rebuilt every frame. (3) `upgradeHuman` keeps one wanted attach per person (a ticket replaced by every rebuild): rebuilds or a man-to-woman change while the kit loads give one clone per person, on the latest group; `attachKit` also refuses a kit for a model the person no longer wants or a person no longer in the scene. (4) A person with swimming off in a pool (their clay floats and bobs at the waterline, which the body can't follow) stays clay: `clayReason` "floats in the water", from `stage._floatsInShot`. (5) `openPhysicsPanel()` with no id and nothing selected returns `{ok: false, error: "select something"}` and opens nothing. (6) Skins no person uses are dropped every pose (deleted, turned into a non-person, or left on an old group), not only when there are more skins than actors: delete + add in one build no longer leaks a clone, mixer and gait table. (8) The selection / hover outline of a lifelike person draws each geometry group with its own material: hair and lash cards keep their cut-out, Faceless's hidden lashes stay hidden, and a Faceless switch rebuilds the outline. (9) `placeInFrame`: `x: true` and other booleans / arrays / empty strings are refused, `"0.3"` is accepted, riding or carried objects are refused, `key_y` is kept when another shot's keys are still raised, and the ray meets the set's raised floor (a prop there is keyed at the floor height, a person's feet follow it). (11) When a live tilt clears, the body stands where the live clay group is for that tick, not at the key; a failed avatar load is shared by the builds of the next 15 s instead of re-requesting the four FBX files on every edit (`setKitRetryMs`); a still or sequence capture says "Waiting for N lifelike people to load" before it waits. (7) The Stage API page is linked from [docs/agent.md](docs/agent.md) and the people README, and lists the new behaviour ([web/stage/STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md)); the knowledge pack is regenerated.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs](tests/js/people_m16.test.mjs) (11 tests: top-down and steep placements, input checks, the raised floor and `key_y`, `openPhysicsPanel()` with no selection, no table rebuild for jitter / bobbing and a bounded number for a 1 m settle, stale attaches with a stand-in loader, the attach refusals, the skin leak, floating with swimming off, outline materials and the Highlighter rebuild, the live-untip position); [tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs](tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs) updated for the 15 s load retry and the waiting note.

**Lifelike people M1.5, review round 3: clay only in the air, raised floors, fresh height tables, the still waits, Stage API fixes (private branch `private/people-m1`)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests only, not in the browser or the App. A jumper now swaps body twice per jump (lifelike to clay at take-off, back at landing): that is what the M1.5 task asked for, and the round-2 "whole shot" rule is gone (it was never approved by Mike); how the swap looks at take-off and landing has not been seen on screen. The obstacle signature now includes each parked object's group transform, so a parked prop whose group moves every frame (none known) would rebuild the height tables every frame. `openPhysicsPanel()` with nothing selected still opens an empty panel and the stale-skin leak on delete + add in one build is still there (both review findings, not in this round's list).
- **Fixed** ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs), [web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs](web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs)): (1) jumps, hops up and vaults show the clay only on the airborne frames (`clayReason` "is in the air (a jump or vault)", from `stage._airborneAt`), lifelike on the ground before and after; the whole-shot `jumps` rule is removed. (2) The "too tall to climb / stand on" cut-off (1.7 m / 1.75 m) in `_heightAt`, `_supportShapes` and the drop-to-climb is measured from the floor under the object (`_floorUnder`, the set's `floorAt`), not from world zero: a 0.8 m crate on the 0.92 m train platform was a wall. (3) `shot.fps` is in `_heightAt`'s signature and in `_jumpsInShot`'s cheap one (gravity per frame depends on it); the obstacle signature (now built without JSON) covers each parked object's clay group transform and identity, `key_y` and the physics lift, and `_jumpsInShot` refreshes it, so moving a crate from the console or a drag, or rebuilding it, refreshes both `_obsCache` and the height / jump tables. (4) `stage.capture(mode)` waits for loading bodies like `captureSequence` (`stage.avatarWaitMs`, the same status message and `lastCaptureNote`) and re-poses them before the render. (5) `setRig` checks `Object.hasOwn(RIGS, type)` and that `params` is a plain object: `setRig('tripod', 1, 'x')` and `setRig('toString')` return `{ok: false, error}` and change nothing. (6) `duplicate` copies the base `keys` (new `_baseKeys`), not the active shot's `by_shot` keys. (7) `placeInFrame` uses the active shot's camera (`_activeShotCamera`, from `_shotCameraAt`), never the live preview in `this.camera`, and with per-shot blocking edits that shot's keys without writing them into the base `position`. [STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md) and the people README say so.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs](tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs): a walker over a crate is lifelike on every ground frame and clay on exactly the airborne ones; a 0.8 m crate on a 0.92 m floor is climbed and stood on, 2 m above it is a wall; fps change, moving the crate's group, re-keying and rebuilding it, and scaling it each refresh the tables with no hand-clearing; `capture` waits, re-poses and reports a timeout; `setRig` with string / number / boolean / array params and inherited names; `duplicate` with per-shot blocking; `placeInFrame` with a preview camera and with per-shot blocking. All of them fail on the round-2 code.

**Lifelike people M1.5, review round 2: walkers no longer pop to clay, live knock-overs, the dust matte, placeInFrame (private branch `private/people-m1`, 15:31)**

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with Node tests only, not re-run in the browser or the App. The live knock-over test drives `live.mjs` `_tick` / `_commit` with a stand-in physics world (a fixed tilted pose), not a real push. `placeInFrame` still searches one screen column assuming the ground gets farther up the frame: under a straight-down camera it doesn't (it passes under the camera), so a short distance such as 1 m is refused as "nearer than any ground the camera sees" although the camera does see it (found by a test; not fixed). The rest of the M1.5 notes below stand.
- **Fixed** ([web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs), [web/stage/live.mjs](web/stage/live.mjs), [web/stage/secondary.mjs](web/stage/secondary.mjs), [web/stage/people/cutout.mjs](web/stage/people/cutout.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs)): (1) M1.5's "tipped" check read the clay group's rotation, so the ordinary gait lean (`gait.mjs` `applyHuman`: up to 0.11 rad running, 0.14 on speed changes, 0.38 into turns) swapped lifelike walkers to clay at walk starts, stops and bends; it now reads a flag `live.mjs` sets from the physics pose (`markLiveTilt`), dropped when the drag is written down. (2) That flag is set after the physics pose goes on the group, re-checking the avatar at once: before, `_silentFrame()` ran the check on the upright group, so the M1.5 claim "a live tilt shows the clay" was not true in the App (a Rotate-gizmo tilt was already covered: it writes `a.tilt` as you drag). (3) The dust matte gave every mesh one black material, so hair and lash cards held the dust out as solid quads and faceless lashes came back; it now keeps the cut-out and hidden groups like Depth / Clay / Wire / ID (`overrideMaterials`). (4) `placeInFrame` returned `ok: true` at the edge of its search when a steep or top-down camera can't reach the distance (asked 10 m, put 3.49 m away): now `{ok: false, error}` saying how far it can go. (6) `_jumpsInShot`, run per person per frame, went through `_heightAt`'s `JSON.stringify` signature each call: now a keys-hash check first. (7) `placeInFrame(id, null)` threw; `STAGE_API.md` now says `openPhysicsPanel` records no undo step (it changes no scene data).
- **Jumps (5), accepted spec:** a person who jumps, hops up or vaults anywhere in the shot stays clay for the whole shot, on the ground too, not only while airborne: clay only in the air would swap the body twice in one shot, which the M1 rule forbids. The browser check that expected "clay only while airborne" is to change to match.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs](tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs) ("round 2"): a keyed walker run through the real stage and gait (start from rest, a 7.2 m/s run, a 90 degree turn, a stop and hold; the clay leans more than 5 degrees) is lifelike on every frame; a knock-over through `live.mjs` `_tick` shows the clay after `setFrame`, stays clay on the next tick, keeps it once `_commit` saves the tilt, and is lifelike again once stood up; the dust matte keeps card map + alphaTest and a hidden group, and puts materials back; a straight-down camera refuses 10 m; `_jumpsInShot` runs no `JSON.stringify` on 100 unchanged calls and still sees a new path or a removed crate; `placeInFrame(id, null)`; no undo step from `openPhysicsPanel`. The two gait / live tests fail on the M1.5 code.

**Lifelike people M1.5: fixes from the M1 review, and four Stage API calls for tools (private branch `private/people-m1`, 15:05)**
- **Still wrong (read first):** checked in a stand-alone browser page that loads the stage (both avatars, close-up), not inside ComfyUI, so the new **Blocking** button beside Wire / Clay / Color / Depth and the Face tooltip were not seen in the App (node syntax check only), and `openPhysicsPanel` was only tested with a stub panel. No GLSL was compiled in node (no GL there): the Depth shader is expanded through three's ShaderChunk with a small preprocessor in the test, and compiled only by the browser. A person who hops up onto anything or vaults is now clay for the WHOLE shot (no pop, but no lifelike jump either). Faceless paints the face one flat skin colour sampled from the cheek: it can sit a shade off the neck (the man's reads greyer) and the normal map still shows the brow ridge and lip line. The lash split is by position near the eyes; brows made of cards far from the eyes would stay when faceless (none in the two installed avatars). A failed avatar load is now retried on every rebuild, so a missing install asks for the files again each time. `placeInFrame` measures `distance_m` along the ground and only knows the flat ground (y = 0), not set floors. Ask's knowledge pack doesn't know the new Stage API: it is written in `web/stage/STAGE_API.md`, not under `docs/`, because any `docs/*.md` change makes the generated pack (`web/agent/`, not this lane's) stale.
- **What** ([web/stage/people/cutout.mjs](web/stage/people/cutout.mjs), [web/stage/depthshader.mjs](web/stage/depthshader.mjs), [web/stage/people/avatars.mjs](web/stage/people/avatars.mjs), [web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs](web/stage/people/avatarcore.mjs), [web/stage/people/peopleui.mjs](web/stage/people/peopleui.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/stage/posepass.mjs](web/stage/posepass.mjs), [web/stage/sets/underwater.mjs](web/stage/sets/underwater.mjs), [web/soundstage.js](web/soundstage.js)): hair and lash cards keep their cut-out in Depth, Clay, Wire and ID (they drew as solid quads: a swapped-in pass material with the card's alpha that the override can't replace; in the browser the solid quads added about 1,100-1,300 px to the close-up's silhouettes); the underwater set's Depth skins; jumps and vaults keep the clay; the physics lift moves the lifelike body; a live, unsaved tilt shows the clay; faceless keeps the hair (scalp texture and hair cards) and drops face landmarks, eyes and ears from the Pose pass; the avatar's height is the clay's built height (a man set to female was 12 cm short); captures wait up to 60 s (`stage.avatarWaitMs`) and say who stayed clay; failed loads aren't cached; a rebuild keeps each person's lifelike body, many attaching cost one re-pose, the gait cache hashes keys instead of JSON, and the unused specular maps are never downloaded; Blocking moved to a scene-level toggle beside the view modes; the Face tooltip is re-read on every hover. New Stage API for tools: `duplicate(id)`, `placeInFrame(id, {x, y, distance_m})`, `setRig` returning `{ok, rig}` / `{ok: false, error}`, `openPhysicsPanel(id)` ([web/stage/STAGE_API.md](web/stage/STAGE_API.md)).
- **Tests:** [tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs](tests/js/avatars_m15.test.mjs) (cut-out pass materials and the swap, ID pass cards, the real files' cards in every pass, the Depth shader expanded through ShaderChunk skinned / not / cut-out / not, underwater Depth, jumps, lift, live tilt, faceless in the Pose pass (clay and real), the face mask, capture wait timeout and retry after a failure, 10 people x 5 rebuilds = no extra setFrame, the four Stage API calls against a known camera, the fresh Face tooltip) and [tests/js/avatars.test.mjs](tests/js/avatars.test.mjs) (height against the clay's built height, lash split and faceless hair on the real files, keys hash, no specular fetch).

**Moving around the set, review follow-ups: the posing Alt line per style, a one-raycast test that can fail, a fly-speed test off the wall clock** (16:25 PT)
- **Still wrong (read first):** the Chrome warning `The specified value "NaN" cannot be parsed, or is out of range.` reported on a fresh try-it load is still not reproduced, so it is not fixed. Headless Chrome over CDP does surface this warning (its `Log` domain showed it for a page that sets a connected number input to NaN on purpose), and it logged none on a fresh load of this branch's build, of the earlier branch build and of the merge-base build, at 1400×900 with SwiftShader and with Metal, plain visit, `#stunt` and `#platform`. The value / attribute / HTML-string hooks saw no NaN in the built-in browser or in Chrome either. Neither browser tool here reports Chrome's own input warnings at all, so where the reviewer saw four is unknown (one candidate: another session's page on 127.0.0.1 was in that tab).
- **Why:** the Tutorial's pose-mode line said Option/Alt-drag looks around, which is wrong in the Maya and Unreal styles (it orbits). The one-raycast-per-press test pressed a grabbable car, so the press took the grab branch and passed with or without the hit reuse. The "inside a car, holding right flies at the tight step" test busy-waited 20 ms of wall clock and failed once under full-suite load.
- **What:** [docs/TUTORIAL.md](docs/TUTORIAL.md) pose mode: Alt-drag moves the camera, never a joint; looks around in Soundstage / Blender, orbits in Maya / Unreal. Knowledge pack regenerated.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs) presses a car that can't be grabbed, checks `nav.begin` ran and the pivot is on the car's face; with the hits not passed to the pivot it fails (2 casts). [tests/js/navigation_stage.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation_stage.test.mjs) injects `performance.now` (exactly 20 ms between frames); it still fails when the tight step is ignored (0.32 m).

**Moving around the set, browser check of the second review round: a mouse's Ctrl + wheel stays 1x at browser zoom or display scaling** (16:06 PT)
- **Still wrong (read first):** the browser tool's wheel ignores modifier keys, so Shift + wheel and Ctrl + wheel were dispatched wheel events (legacy `wheelDeltaX/Y` set on the event), not a real held key; Option + W was a real key press, but a held W with Option pressed mid-hold was dispatched key events. The set-piece double-click used the Stunt car with `set_piece` set from the console (this demo has no set piece but the buildings, which are set boxes and also keep the direction). The interior was built with `stage.setInterior` from the console. Chrome's warning `The specified value "NaN" cannot be parsed, or is out of range.` (4x) shows on a fresh try-it load of this branch and of a merge-base build alike in the built-in browser, before any input; it does not come from the navigation panel's inputs and is not traced. A trackpad stream that starts within 600 ms of mouse notches still takes up to 6 events before it orbits (the first ones zoom). Real devices, Safari and Windows are unchecked.
- **Why:** at 1400×900 emulated in a smaller pane the page's wheel deltas are fractional while `wheelDelta` stays a 120-step notch (as at a browser zoom other than 100% or some display scalings). `classifyWheel` took any fractional Ctrl + wheel for a pinch, so a mouse's Ctrl + wheel zoomed 5x there (19.1 m for a notch the plain wheel moved 4.3 m).
- **What** ([web/stage/navigation.mjs](web/stage/navigation.mjs)): Ctrl + wheel is a pinch from a trackpad, or with fractional deltas that are not a notch.
- **Checked live** (try-it built from this working tree, 127.0.0.1, 1400×900): a real drag starting on a building wall orbited about the wall point (pivot on the wall face, 0.2 px drift on a sideways drag); a real double-click on the floor 2.2 m ahead, with the camera 0.24 m from the room's back wall, moved only the target (the old point focus would have put the camera outside); a real double-click on the set-piece car kept the view direction (dot 1.0) with the target on the clicked surface, while the plain car was framed; Shift + wheel notches (macOS deltaX and Windows forms) stayed mouse, also right after a trackpad stream; Ctrl + wheel notches, integer and fractional, now zoom the same as the plain wheel and a real pinch still zooms 5x; real W then Option + W left nothing held, and dispatched W / Option orders (Option mid-hold, Option first, keyup as ∑) all let go and the camera stopped.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/navigation.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation.test.mjs) (a fractional Ctrl + wheel notch at 110% zoom and mid-stream is the mouse); both fail on the old code.

**Moving around the set, second review round: a far double-click frames again, the mouse wheel comes back after a trackpad, hidden or moved set pieces don't catch the cursor** (15:55 PT)
- **Still wrong (read first):** the console warning `The specified value "NaN" cannot be parsed, or is out of range.` reported on a fresh try-it load was not reproduced or found. A hook on every way a page can set an input's value (the `value`, `valueAsNumber`, `defaultValue`, `min` / `max` / `step` setters, `setAttribute`, `innerHTML` / `outerHTML` / `insertAdjacentHTML`, and inserted inputs) saw no NaN on a fresh load of this branch's build or of a `main` build, plain visit or `#stunt`, at 1400×900. Neither headless Chrome over CDP nor the browser tools here surface Chrome's own NaN-input warning (a page that sets one on purpose logs nothing), so it may come from a saved setting in the reviewer's browser; it is not traced to this branch. The wheel heuristics are still modelled, not recorded from real devices.
- **Why:** a review found that double-click on an object more than ~50 m away did nothing (it went through the orbit pivot's distance cap); after a trackpad it took 6 mouse notches to zoom again (the first 5 orbited) and a pause never reset it; the sightline check overwrote the stage's shared pick ray; a hidden set piece still caught the pivot and blocked a double-click, and the set-collider cache never noticed a set piece moving; the Drive and gizmo-chord tests called `nav` directly instead of going through the stage's handlers.
- **What** ([web/stage/navigation.mjs](web/stage/navigation.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs)): double-click takes any surface hit at any distance (the floor within 400 m); only the orbit pivot keeps the cap. A pause of 600 ms between wheel events starts a new gesture with the evidence back at neutral; a 120-step notch (or line mode) that starts a gesture switches to the mouse at once, two in a row switch mid-stream (one fast trackpad flick that reads as a notch still doesn't). The sightline and glide checks cast their own raycaster. Set colliders carry their mesh (non-enumerable `src`) and a hidden one is skipped; the cache key includes each set group's position, rotation and scale. The stage's own wheel and WASD step do nothing while Drive is on (Drive already swaps them out; this keeps the view still if they ever see an event).
- **Checked live** (try-it built from this working tree, served on 127.0.0.1, 1400×900): a real double-click on the Stunt car from 80 m with the target 10 m out framed it. Dispatched wheel events: 4 trackpad events, a pause, then a macOS-style notch read as mouse on the first notch; with no pause the second notch; after a pause the trackpad needs 3 events; line mode is a mouse at once. A real wheel scroll after a trackpad stream zoomed (view direction kept).
- **Tests:** [tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs) (an object ~80 m away is framed while the orbit pivot still ignores it, and a far set wall takes the target; the pick ray is unchanged after a blocked double-click; a hidden set-piece wall neither catches the pivot nor stops framing the car behind it; moving a set piece refreshes the cache; Drive through the stage's real wheel, key and double-click handlers leaves the camera exactly where it was; both buttons on pointermove while the gizmo drags stay an orbit). The first five fail on the old code. [tests/js/navigation.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation.test.mjs) (trackpad then mouse after a pause and mid-stream; idle-gap decay).

**Moving around the set, navigation review fixes: held keys can't stick with Option, the orbit pivot and double-click see walls and set buildings, a set piece double-click doesn't frame the whole set, mouse / trackpad detection needs several events, a mouse's Ctrl + wheel zooms at 1x** (15:20 PT)
- **Still wrong (read first):** none of this round was checked in a browser; it is node tests on the stage's real pointer, key and wheel handlers (`Stage.prototype` stub, hand-driven clock and frames). The wheel heuristics are modelled on documented Chrome / Safari / Firefox behaviour, not recorded from real devices: a macOS accelerated mouse is assumed to report 120-step `wheelDeltaY` notches. Auto-detect now needs three trackpad-like events in a row (more after a known mouse), so the first two or three scrolls of a trackpad zoom before it orbits; ⚙ Scrolling: Trackpad skips that. Shift does not drop the held keys (it is the 4x speed), so a Shift keyup elsewhere is still matched by e.code only. A double-click whose framing of an object would cross a wall falls back to the point focus, and the wall test covers set boxes and interior walls only (people and cars are glided past as before).
- **Why:** a review found eight defects: held WASD stuck when Option changed `e.key` (W -> ∑) before the keyup; the orbit pivot and double-click only raycast objects, so they went through walls, set buildings and interior walls (a double-click inside a room could back the camera out through the wall behind); a double-click on a set piece framed the whole set; the mouse / trackpad guess flipped on one event and read Shift + wheel and tilt wheels (deltaX) as a trackpad; a mouse's Ctrl + wheel zoomed 5x as if it were a pinch; the Tutorial's tables didn't describe both-buttons pan, hold-right fly, double-click, trackpad, held keys or the styles; each press raycast the objects twice; and some guards had no tests.
- **What** ([web/stage/navigation.mjs](web/stage/navigation.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs)): held keys are tracked by `e.code` and let go on window blur, a hidden tab, the pointer leaving the window and any ⌘ / Option / Ctrl press. The pivot and the double-click take the nearest of the objects, the set colliders (`_setCollidersCached`, ray vs oriented box `rayBoxT`) and the interior's meshes, skipping walls and ceilings the dollhouse cutaway hides (`_cutawayHidden`, now shared with the render). A double-click whose glide or sightline crosses a wall moves along the click ray instead (`focusAlongRay`). A set piece double-click moves the target to the clicked point and keeps the view direction. `classifyWheel` adds up evidence (120-step legacy notches = mouse, fractional pixel deltas or non-notch legacy deltas = trackpad, line mode = mouse at once, deltaX alone = nothing) and changes kind only once sure; Ctrl + wheel is a pinch only from a trackpad or with fractional deltas. The stage's actor raycast on a press is handed to the pivot.
- **Docs:** [docs/TUTORIAL.md](docs/TUTORIAL.md) "The mouse" and the cheat sheet (both-buttons pan, hold-right fly, held keys, double-click, trackpad, the four styles and what Alt does in each, the ⚙ settings); the knowledge pack is regenerated.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs) (Option while holding W; keyup by code; blur / pointer out / Ctrl / ⌘; pivot on a wall; a cut-away interior wall looked through; a double-click in a room never through the wall behind; set piece focus; mouse Ctrl + wheel 1x vs pinch 5x; one actor raycast per press; RotateTools' Alt guard in all four styles; Maya / Unreal Alt over a gizmo arrow; no key written by a Camera-view double-click; a key camera keeps its double-click; nothing while driving; the gizmo drag ignores the chord) and [tests/js/navigation.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation.test.mjs) (Shift + wheel and tilt wheels stay a mouse; a macOS smooth mouse with an odd event stays a mouse; `rayBoxT`; `focusAlongRay`).

**Moving around the set, third live pass: a drag that arrives in one jump no longer flings the view** (14:53 PT)
- **Still wrong (read first):** checked in the try-it build in the built-in browser (Chrome, 1440×900). The browser tool's own mouse drags are a press, one jump and a release, so the drags that check exact pixels were dispatched pointer events with 16 ms steps. The both-buttons chord, hold-right fly, pinch and middle / Alt+right drags were synthetic too. How it feels by hand, Safari, Windows and real trackpads are still unchecked. A real human flick glides as strongly as before (about 160 px of extra orbit for a release at 1 px/ms). A press on a car you haven't selected grabs it and moves it (that is the Milestone 9 grab, not navigation), so a first drag that starts on a car moves the car, not the view.
- **Why:** the flick speed is an average over move samples, and the first sample assumes 16 ms. A drag that reached the page as one jump (the browser tool's drags, and likely some remote-desktop and tablet drivers) read as a fast flick: a 37 px real drag swung the free view about 75° and down to the floor limit.
- **What** ([web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs)): a release glides on only after at least 3 move samples.
- **Checked live** (screenshots before and after, kept outside the repo): after the fix the same 37 px real drag orbited a few degrees with the grabbed floor point on its pixel. Orbit about the floor point (0.1 px), the wheel zooming toward the car (0.1 px), pinch in and out (back to the same distance), double-click on the car (target = the car's centre, the car didn't move), `F`, both buttons on empty floor and on the car (the car stayed put, the grabbed point ended 1 px from the cursor), hold right + W (3.7 m in 1 s at 4 m/s) and + D with the car selected (flew right, Drive stayed off, no context menu), each style's buttons (Blender middle orbit / Shift+middle pan / Ctrl+middle dolly; Maya Alt+left orbit, also by real mouse / Alt+middle pan / Alt+right dolly; Unreal left drag turn and walk / Alt+right dolly; both buttons pan in all four), the style picker on the hint, the compass click (top view) and drag, a click selecting the car, the gizmo X arrow (moved along X only, camera still), a right click without a drag, and a Camera-view drag and flick writing the shot camera once each. No console errors.
- **Tests:** a jump-versus-flick case in [tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs); it fails on the old code.

**Moving around the set, review fixes: held keys can't stick on a Mac, Camera-view moves are written exactly once, a sky double-click does nothing, the hint keeps clear of the Top view** (14:42 PT)
- **Still wrong (read first):** none of this round was checked in a browser; it comes from reading the code and from node tests that run the stage's real pointer, key and wheel handlers on a `Stage.prototype` stub (hand-driven clock and frames). The Mac ⌘ keyup gap is Chrome's documented behaviour, but the fix was not tried on a real Mac. The hint placement was not seen on screen. A flick still glides as strongly as before (it now also carries the drag's unfinished smoothing instead of running on top of it). The regenerated knowledge pack (`web/agent/knowledge_index.mjs`, from commit 565d23a) still needs Mike's OK, since that path belongs to the other lane.
- **Why:** a review found six defects. (1) On macOS, Chrome sends no keyup for a key let go while ⌘ is down, so a held W stayed held and the camera (the shot camera in Camera view) flew on by itself. (2) Pressing the canvas during a 300 ms compass or numpad snap stopped the shot camera part-way and never saved it. (3) A damped drag in Camera view saved the shot camera twice, and a flick's glide ran on top of the drag's leftover smoothing. (4) Double-clicking the sky pulled a far camera in to 12 m of the old target. (5) The hint bar sat on top of the Top view inset. (6) Maya / Unreal Alt+right dolly also turned on fly mode, so the wheel changed fly speed and D flew instead of starting Drive.
- **What** ([web/stage/navigation.mjs](web/stage/navigation.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs)): ⌘ (keydown, keyup with ⌘ held, a hidden tab, a window blur) drops every held fly key; a press that cuts an eased jump short writes the shot camera where it stopped; a release whose drag is still easing in leaves the single write to the frame loop, and a flick's glide takes over that leftover; a double-click with nothing under the cursor does nothing (`pickPivot` with `fallback: false`); the hint is placed by `hintPlacement`, which measures the shot-camera picture and the Top view inset and sits between them or above them; an Alt+right press is never fly mode. While there: the flick glide now honours Invert Y the way the drag does.
- **Tests:** new [tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation_stagehooks.test.mjs) (both buttons on the selected car write no position key; a click under 5 px selects and an empty click deselects; a right click selects and replays one context menu; right held + D never calls `drive.start`; an eased snap marks history; the interrupted snap, damped drag and flick each write once; sky double-click; ⌘Z while holding W; Maya Alt+right). Three of them fail on the old `stage.mjs`. `bindingConflicts` now checks each style against the real `SHORTCUTS` combos, with a test showing it catches a clash.

**Moving around the set, second live pass: a pan keeps the grabbed point under the cursor near the frame edge too** (14:25)
- **Still wrong (read first):** the second live pass ran in the try-it build in the built-in browser. Its page only drew frames while it was on screen, at about 1 to 19 frames a second. The both-buttons chord, hold-right fly, pinch, trackpad scrolls and the style switches were synthetic pointer, wheel and key events. Orbit, zoom, double-click, `F`, the object drag, the gizmo drag, the compass and Maya's Alt+drag were real mouse input. Feel by hand, Safari, Windows and real trackpads are still unchecked. A quick flick with damping on keeps gliding for about 0.16 s at the release speed: an 88 px flick orbited about 160°, down to the floor limit. That is how the glide was designed, but it may feel too strong. Escape doesn't close the Navigation settings panel (the ⚙ does). The demo has a second, hidden stage whose hint doesn't change to a new style until it reloads (the style itself is shared through storage). Holding right with W from a standstill covered 4.35 m in 2 s at 4 m/s, because of the ease-in.
- **Why:** a pan moved the camera by the grabbed point's straight-line distance, not by its depth along the view. Off-centre the point ran ahead of the cursor: about 10% at 300 px from the centre, more on far floor.
- **What** ([web/stage/navigation.mjs](web/stage/navigation.mjs) `viewDepth`, [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs) `_navDrag`): the pan rate uses the depth along the view direction.
- **Checked live:** after the fix, the grabbed floor point ended within 0.5 px of the cursor for a both-buttons pan and a Shift pan in Free view, both near the centre and near the edge. In Camera view it ended within 1 px. Before the fix it ended 13 to 18 px off. Re-checked with no change needed: the orbit pivot stayed on its pixel, the wheel and pinch zoomed toward the cursor point, the two-finger scroll orbited and Shift+scroll panned. Also double-click focus, `F`, hold right + W flying forward along the view, the wheel setting fly speed while flying, and the context menu staying shut after a right-drag. Each style's buttons worked (Blender middle orbit / Ctrl+middle dolly, Maya Alt+left orbit / Alt+right dolly, Unreal left walk), and so did the compass click (top view) and drag. Dragging the selected car moved it and left the camera alone, and the gizmo X arrow moved it along X only. No console errors (only the favicon 404).
- **Tests:** a `viewDepth` case in [tests/js/navigation.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation.test.mjs).

- **Still wrong (read first):** checked with node tests that dispatch real capture / bubble events at the navigation controller with Drive's own listeners (drive.mjs `_bind`, unchanged) attached, not on a full WebGL Stage; the Windows context-menu order and the off-canvas release are simulated, not tried on a Windows PC.
- **Why (review findings 1, 6, 10, 12):** a right press while driving set fly mode, and Drive's window-capture `pointerup` swallowed the release, so after Drive the wheel changed fly speed instead of zooming and D flew instead of starting Drive (the same after a right press on the gizmo released off the canvas); holding right inside a car flew at the set's fly speed (4 m/s) and left the car; on Windows a both-buttons pan released left-first opened the Compare bar's Fix menu; and in Camera view a two-finger orbit followed quickly by a click moved the shot key without telling the node.

**Moving around the set, checked in a live browser: Alt over a gizmo arrow orbits in Maya / Unreal, the hint no longer covers the Drop pill, flying keeps its speed on a slow GPU**
- **Still wrong (read first):** one live pass in the try-it build (Chrome, the built-in browser, 3 to 16 frames a second), with real mouse drags, clicks, double-clicks, scrolls and keys; the both-buttons chord, hold-right fly, pinch and two-finger trackpad scrolls were synthetic events, so how they feel by hand is still unchecked, and so are Safari and real trackpads. Even with the new 0.25 s frame cap, flying at 3 fps covered about half the set speed. A pan grabbed on far floor (30 m) drifts about 18 px from the cursor because the rate uses distance, not depth. The hint line is now right-aligned and cut short with an ellipsis (hover shows all of it). The settings panel has no close button (the ⚙ toggles it). Once, right after selecting the car by code, an Alt+drag in Unreal style moved and turned the car; it didn't happen again in eight tries.
- **Why:** in Maya and Unreal styles an Alt+drag that started on a gizmo arrow slid the object along it instead of orbiting; the hint line sat on top of the "Drop a video here" pill and the shot-camera picture; the style could only be changed from the hint, so hiding it hid the style too; and each frame counted at most 0.05 s, so on a heavy set flying crawled at a fraction of the set speed.
- **What** ([web/stage/navigation.mjs](web/stage/navigation.mjs), [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [docs/navigation.md](docs/navigation.md)): Alt+drag on a gizmo arrow orbits in Maya / Unreal (Soundstage and Blender still turn the object); the hint is right-aligned with room left for the pills; a Style row in the settings panel; frames count up to 0.25 s.
- **Checked live:** orbit about the clicked point (the point stayed on the same pixel), zoom to the cursor (same), double-click on the floor and on the car, `F`, hold right + W (the context menu stayed shut, D flew instead of Drive), the wheel's fly speed, both buttons pan on empty space and on the selected car (the car stayed put), pinch, trackpad orbit and Shift pan, every style's buttons and keys (Blender Numpad 1 / Ctrl+1 / 7 / Home, Maya Shift+A), the compass click and drag, click to select and clear, right click selects, object drag, gizmo drag, damping off, invert Y, the style kept after a reload. No console errors.

**Moving around the set: both buttons pan, hold right to fly, orbit about the cursor, double-click to glide there**
- **Still wrong (read first):** checked by unit tests only. Nobody has tried it in a live App or a browser, so how the chord, flying, damping and the eased compass feel is unverified, and so is the hint line's placement (it isn't measured against the picture-in-picture monitor or the Top view inset, so it may sit over them). Trackpad detection is a guess: a smooth-scrolling mouse can be taken for a trackpad, and then scrolling orbits instead of zooming (Navigation… › Scrolling › Mouse wheel fixes it). Safari's pinch (`gesturechange`) is wired but untested. No pivot dot is drawn while orbiting. Right-drag used to orbit; it now looks around in place (fly), which is a change for anyone used to it.
- **Why:** the orbit swung round a point you couldn't see after a zoom, both buttons together did nothing, WASD moved in key-repeat steps, the wheel's easing ran twice as fast at 120 Hz, and there was no double-click focus, fly mode or familiar Blender / Maya / Unreal bindings.
- **What** ([web/stage/navigation.mjs](web/stage/navigation.mjs), hook lines in [web/stage/stage.mjs](web/stage/stage.mjs), [web/stage/viewnav.mjs](web/stage/viewnav.mjs), [web/stage/rotate.mjs](web/stage/rotate.mjs), [web/stage/camviz.mjs](web/stage/camviz.mjs), [web/soundstage.js](web/soundstage.js), [docs/navigation.md](docs/navigation.md)):
  - A drag orbits about the object or floor under the cursor; a pan keeps the grabbed point under the pointer. Close in or inside a car it still looks around in place.
  - Left and right together pan. A press on an object that hasn't started moving it becomes a pan, the object stays put, and it is never a click. Gizmo, live, light and pose drags ignore it.
  - Hold right to fly: the mouse looks and W A S D Q E steer (D is fly right then, not Drive); the wheel sets the speed. A right click without a drag still selects and still opens the Identical? bar's Fix menu (the menu event is held back and replayed).
  - W A S D Q E glide while held instead of stepping on key repeat; the shot key is written once when the motion stops.
  - Double-click glides to what you clicked (Free view only; in the Camera view it only selects), and ↶ Last view returns from it. A key camera keeps its own click.
  - The wheel's easing is the same at any frame rate; a pinch zooms; on a trackpad two fingers orbit (Shift pans).
  - The compass eases to an axis (0.3 s), names each view on hover, and a drag on it orbits.
  - Navigation… (on the viewport) and the ⚙ on the new hint line: Soundstage, Blender, Maya or Unreal style, damping, smoothness, invert Y, scrolling mode, fly speed, kept per browser. Reduced motion turns damping off. In Maya and Unreal styles Alt+drag orbits instead of turning an object.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/navigation.test.mjs](tests/js/navigation.test.mjs) (styles, chord, orbit keeps the pivot on screen and never flips, pivot order, frame-rate independent easing, wheel classification, fly, focus, settings, the D-means-Drive rule, one key write per move, Drive left alone), [tests/js/viewnav.test.mjs](tests/js/viewnav.test.mjs) (a double-click focus is a jump).
**Models panel: download the App's model files from Hugging Face inside the App (private/model-downloader)**
- **Still wrong (read first):** nothing has been downloaded from Hugging Face. Every test runs against a fake HTTP layer, so these are assumptions the fake encodes: `X-Linked-Etag`/`X-Linked-Size` on a resolve HEAD, 401 vs 403 for gated repos, and the `X-Error-Code` values. The catalog has no sha256 values or pinned revisions (so sha256 is checked only when Hugging Face sends an `X-Linked-Etag`; otherwise a file is checked by size only, against the catalog's rounded GB figure when no exact size comes back, and a file with no known size at all is kept unchecked as `verified: "none"`), its sizes are the rounded GB figures from MODELS.md, and two Krea 2 sizes are unknown. Only MiniMax H3's first/last-frame file is listed. Ask's knowledge pack is regenerated with `node tools/build_knowledge.mjs` (generated files only; no hand edits in `web/agent/**`). The notice button is added from outside `web/ui/modelguard.mjs`. The panel was checked in a browser against a fake API, not in a live ComfyUI.
- **What** ([soundstage/modeldl/](soundstage/modeldl/), [web/models/](web/models/), [docs/models.md](docs/models.md)): project menu → **Models…** and a **Get these models…** button on the missing-model notice. Files are grouped by feature, with size, licence and state; "Using your build" reuses the notice's matching. There is one licence confirm per selection, an access check per repo, and one download at a time into `.part`. Downloads resume, verify sha256 and size, then rename, with a disk check and cancel. The HF token is stored once at mode 600 and is never returned or logged. It goes only to huggingface.co, never to the CDN redirect. Paths come only from the catalog and are checked to stay inside the models folder.
- **With no server** (the try-it demo, or ComfyUI is down) the panel shows the catalog read-only instead of an error: groups, sizes, licences and, for gated files, the token and accept-the-licence links, with a note that checking and downloading need the App running in ComfyUI. The catalog moved to [web/models/catalog.json](web/models/catalog.json) so the server and the panel read the same file. Checked in the try-it demo (desktop and phone width, no console errors); the Hugging Face links are the catalog's repos and weren't opened.
- **Review fixes:** a download with no sha256 anywhere is now size-checked (it was installed with no check at all). "Installed" needs the size to match, so a truncated file with the right name shows **Wrong size: download again** instead of being skipped. A resume checks the server's `Content-Range` before appending, and a complete `.part` is finished instead of thrown away and fetched again. In the panel: a job that fails before the first progress poll (disk, sign-in) now shows its reason, also on reopening; a ticked "Using your build" row no longer leaves a confirm whose Download can't be pressed; a failed access check says so instead of freezing; Cancel stops only the current file; and one failed refresh mid-download no longer drops to the offline list.
- **Keyboard fix (15:52):** opening the panel from the project menu left focus on the App's project button behind it, so Space or Enter (whose default action the key guard lets through) opened the App's project menu, drawn above the Models panel with clickable items. The panel now takes focus when it opens and gives it back on close, and a key pressed while focus is somewhere behind the dialog does nothing but bring focus into it. Checked in the try-it demo (no server, plus a fake in-page API for the token states): Tab wraps both ways, H, Tab, Cmd+Z and Space never reach the App, Esc closes and focus returns to the project button. Still not checked in a live ComfyUI.
- **Second review fixes:** a good file is no longer judged by the catalog's rounded GB figure. "Wrong size: download again" now needs an exact size (a catalog size with its sha256 filled, or the size recorded in `user/soundstage/models_verified.json` when the App verified its own download); otherwise the file reads **Installed**, with "(size differs from the estimate)" when it is more than 5 MB off. This stops a correct 21 GB file being pre-ticked and fetched again in a loop. A model file symlinked into the models folder from a shared or network volume now counts as present and is size-checked through the link; only the write path (the resolved target directory and the `.part`) is checked for escape, and a symlinked `.part` is still never written through.
- **Third review fixes (15:15):**
  - **Still wrong (read first):** still nothing downloaded from the real Hugging Face. The catalog sizes were NOT filled: the catalog notes hold no Hugging Face API sizes, and fetching them is a download the maintainer hasn't approved. So a download that gets no `X-Linked-Size`/`X-Linked-Etag` (a proxy that strips them) is held to the catalog's rounded figure within 1 %, and for small files (`flux2-vae` 0.34 GB, the 0.26 GB temporal upscaler) the 0.01 GB rounding alone can exceed 1 % and refuse a good file. Real Hugging Face LFS answers carry both headers, so this only bites without them. ComfyUI's own window key listeners that load before the panel module still see keys first. The try-it demo was not re-run in Chrome (`tools/tryit/test_tryit.mjs`); the 404 fix is tested by unit test only.
  - A model already in a second models folder (`extra_model_paths.yaml`) read **Missing** and downloaded again. Now every folder ComfyUI lists for the type is checked, and an exact-name file in any of them counts as installed (server and panel). Downloads still go to the first folder.
  - A response's own `Content-Length` no longer counts as "verified". Without a sha256 or `X-Linked-Size`, the size must be within 1 % of the catalog figure, or it is reported `unverified_size`; either way the file must start like a safetensors file (8-byte little-endian header length, then a JSON object). A `text/html` (any `text/*`) answer, such as a Wi-Fi sign-in page, is refused before anything is written. A cut-off transfer keeps its `.part`. A resume sends `If-Range` with the ETag (or Last-Modified) noted when the `.part` started (`<name>.part.meta`); if the server answers 200 the file starts over cleanly. A `.part` with neither a note nor a sha256 starts over.
  - Token: the panel says which token is in use (saved / `HF_TOKEN` / huggingface-cli) and lets you paste a different one, which is saved and wins; **Remove token** says what it goes back to. A 401 with a token on an ungated repo now says "Your Hugging Face token was refused: replace it above", not "can't find this file". The token moved to its own folder `user/soundstage/hf/token` (folder 700, file 600); the shared `user/soundstage` folder is no longer chmodded. It is written via a unique temp file opened with `O_EXCL` at 600, then renamed. A token at the old `hf_token` path is still read and moved on the next save.
  - Access is checked per file, not per repo; only "ok", "sign in" and "accept the licence" are cached (60 s), errors are asked again. The one Downloader is made at register time (and under a lock otherwise), so two first requests can't make two. Cancel during the hashing of a `.part` no longer starts the GET; `discard` works for queued files; a changed `catalog.json` reaches the Downloader (an invalid one is ignored and the last good one kept).
  - The panel stops every key at the window (capture phase, added when the module loads, before the App's own listeners) and at the dialog root, so Tab, H, Cmd+Z and Space no longer reach the App; Tab wraps inside the dialog and Esc closes it. In the try-it demo the panel goes straight to the read-only list without asking a server (that request logged a 404).
  - The real `Transport` is now tested against two local aiohttp servers (a Hugging Face stand-in that redirects, a CDN stand-in on another port with Range / If-Range / ETag). The no-leak test covers a network failure and `whoami` raising.
- **Tests:** [tests/test_modeldl_catalog.py](tests/test_modeldl_catalog.py), [tests/test_modeldl_download.py](tests/test_modeldl_download.py), [tests/test_modeldl_routes.py](tests/test_modeldl_routes.py), [tests/test_modeldl_transport.py](tests/test_modeldl_transport.py), [tests/js/modelplan.test.mjs](tests/js/modelplan.test.mjs).

## 2026-11-06

**✦ Ask no longer loads its model when the App opens**
- **Still wrong (read first):** tested with a fake Ollama, not the real model, so how long the first answer takes now is unmeasured. A turn that has to load gets up to 4.5 minutes instead of 90 s; past that it gives up, the load carries on and the next question is quicker. Stop ends the wait, not the load. A retry during a load may not show the loading note, because Ollama can list a model before it has finished loading. The CPU tier still keeps the model in RAM during a render until the idle limit, because only the GPU tier unloads before one.
- **Why:** opening the App sent a warm-up, and every request kept the model loaded forever, so one page view held 18 GB until someone unloaded it by hand. That broke the rule that Ask's model stays off during renders.
- **What** ([soundstage/assistant.py](soundstage/assistant.py), [web/agent/director.mjs](web/agent/director.mjs), [web/agent/directorpanel.mjs](web/agent/directorpanel.mjs), [tools/start_ollama.sh](tools/start_ollama.sh)): the warm-up is gone and the model loads on the first question. It unloads after 10 idle minutes (`SOUNDSTAGE_ASSISTANT_KEEP_ALIVE` changes that), and the server sets this, so a tab left open from an older version can't pin the model. A question that has to load the model gets more time and says so after 3 seconds. Expect a slow answer after a break.
- **Tests:** [tests/test_assistant.py](tests/test_assistant.py) (a fake Ollama records every request: status only reads, the chat sends the limit), [tests/js/assistant_brain.test.mjs](tests/js/assistant_brain.test.mjs).
**14:10 PST · Shipped workflows load on ComfyUI 0.44: `CreateVideo` no longer stores its defaults**
- **Still wrong (read first):** a workflow someone already saved on an older ComfyUI (their own copy, or one embedded in a render) still holds the old four values and still fails on 0.44+ until `chroma_subsampling` is set back to `auto` by hand; this change is data only, so nothing patches old files at load. If ComfyUI later inserts another input before `bit_depth`, the `8` kept in the Decode subgraph lands in the wrong widget again; the rule here cuts the risk, it doesn't remove it. Nothing was rendered: both test servers ran CPU-only with no model files and without other node packs, so whole-graph validation stops at those missing nodes and I validated each `CreateVideo` on its own. Checked on 0.43.2 with frontend 1.57.4 and 0.44.2 with frontend 1.57.6 only. `widgets_values_named` still lists the old values; it is by name, so it's correct, and the 0.44.2 load ignored it.
- **What** ([example_workflows/](example_workflows/), [tools/app_build/audio.py](tools/app_build/audio.py)): UI workflows store widget values by position. 0.44.0 added `chroma_subsampling` before `codec`, so a real load on 0.44.2 put the stored `"none"` into it, and `/prompt` refused all 32 `CreateVideo` instances across the 8 workflows that have one (`'none' not in ['auto', '444', '422', '420']`). Those workflows now store `[fps]`, or `[fps, 8]` in the Decode subgraph where 8-bit isn't the default, and the App builder writes the same. No code, version check or migration changed. Real loads now show `auto`/`sRGB`/`none` from the widgets' own defaults: identical to before on 0.43.2, `chroma_subsampling: auto` on 0.44.2, no `CreateVideo` validation errors on either. The App's API graphs leave the optional inputs out: a Loop prompt from its builder validated with no `CreateVideo` errors on both, the only failures being the absent models, after swapping in a core node for one absent LTX pack helper.
- **Tests:** [tests/test_workflow_createvideo_defaults.py](tests/test_workflow_createvideo_defaults.py) (shipped workflows and subgraphs store no trailing default, the builder doesn't write them back), and the existing builder test still reproduces the committed App.

**✦ Ask tuned from real use, and a director's breakdown of each scene (task 2)**
- **Still wrong (read first):** tested with one invented 10-scene script, on the CPU tier only (16 GB card), not a feature-length script and not the GPU tier. The director's reading is a local model's interpretation: its line citations are checked, its judgement isn't, and it can read a secret into a character that the scene only hints at. Two scenes per turn, so a long script takes many Next presses. "Put Jane in the booth" still plans "a jane": the set planner doesn't know the script's cast. See the private misses log.
- **Measured:** 36 asks with `qwen3:30b-a3b` in the App, re-run in rounds after the fixes. Before: invented plot, invented buttons and values, a plan to bring in rain on "hi". The list of each miss and its fix is in the misses log.
- **What** ([web/agent/scenebook.mjs](web/agent/scenebook.mjs), [web/agent/assistant.mjs](web/agent/assistant.mjs), [web/agent/charbuild.mjs](web/agent/charbuild.mjs), [web/agent/copilot.mjs](web/agent/copilot.mjs), [web/agent/ask.mjs](web/agent/ask.mjs)):
  - `break_down_scene`: character, prop, location, emotion, tone, the turn and a staging idea, script facts kept apart from the reading, one button to add it to the Director's Book.
  - The model now gets the script's own words for the scene or person asked about.
  - Tools run only when the words ask for them; known problems, recipes and docs answer before the model can guess.
  - Who-is and build questions go straight to the profile. A paragraph about one person counts as about them, and a nickname given in it resolves ("the tall stranger ... Everyone calls him PAT").
  - A card change shows what it will write, and the yes writes it. Answers have more room and never show raw JSON.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/scenebook.test.mjs](tests/js/scenebook.test.mjs), plus the existing Ask tests, three of them changed on purpose (the build goes straight to the profile, a known problem goes to the playbook first, a check's card replaces the model's reply).

**A live job timer under the top row and a soft done-chime**
- **Still wrong (read first):** no browser, GPU or speakers here: the chime's notes, gains and timing are checked as data and with a fake audio context, so nobody has heard it, and how soft it is on real speakers or headphones is untested. The percent is the Activity bar's own (time against your average, else node and step count), so on a kind you've never run it is a step or node count, and the time left is a range until three runs are measured. A job from another browser tab shows the clock but no step or node, and never chimes. Browsers keep audio locked until your first click or key press on the page, so a job that ends before that is silent (the pill pulse and the tab title still show). No browser notification.
- **What** ([web/ui/jobTimer.mjs](web/ui/jobTimer.mjs), new; [web/ui/activity.mjs](web/ui/activity.mjs)): while a job runs, a slim bar under the top row shows its name, a big `mm:ss` counter that ticks every second, the percent, `step 6 / 12`, the time left ("about 2–3 min left" while the average is thin, "at least" when only the step rate is known), the node it is on, and "2 more queued · about 7 min total". A loader or VAE decode, with no step info, gets an indeterminate bar and the clock only, never a made-up percent. When a job of yours finishes, a soft two-or-three-note rising chime (sine, slow attack, long decay, under a second, quiet by default, capped well below full scale) plays; a stopped or failed job gets a gentler falling pair. The pill pulses once and, if the tab is in the background, its title reads "✓ Done" until you come back. Settings are in the Activity panel under **Sound** (on/off, volume, only when the tab is in the background, only for jobs longer than N seconds, default 15, and Test buttons), saved in this browser, and softer again under reduced-motion.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/jobTimer.test.mjs](tests/js/jobTimer.test.mjs) (11): the formatter, percent, step and time-left words (thin average, at-least, unknown), the chime as data (notes, lengths, gains within the safe max), settings persistence with a blocked store, the when-to-chime rule, and a fake-DOM run that the timer ticks and that the chime fires once per finished job, not on every event.

**✦ Ask acts: plans of real App actions you lock in, run step by step on what each step did, self-checked, one Undo; "Do it for me" really runs**
- **Still wrong (read first):** never run with a real model, in a browser or on a pod: the loop is tested with stubbed replies and a fake App (stage, Director, ② Cast panel, ⑤ Motion), so how well the local model or Qwen3-30B-A3B follows the plan / observe protocol is unmeasured, and the Director's backends cap an answer at ~400 tokens, which a long plan can overrun (one re-ask, then the playbook). The new hooks in [web/soundstage.js](web/soundstage.js) (`_setAdherence`, `ltx.patch` / `ltx.setPreset`, the Follow the clay and Length sliders, `cut.setTransition`, `stage.select`) are wired, not clicked through. `stage_order` / `add_shot` depend on the Director's planner understanding the words. J/L cuts stay click-steps (the Cut has no split edit).
- **Root cause:** Ask knew the fixes and the recipes but couldn't do them: "Do it for me" handed the Director one sentence ("apply the gunshot preset") or nothing at all (the clay fix is three ⑤ Motion settings, not a set change), multi-part requests ("re-block this chase, add a crash, then cover it in three shots") became one set order, and nothing checked what a change actually did.

The plan runs as an agent loop (think, call, OBSERVE, decide; one retry per failed step; never outside the plan; `MAX_STEPS` 12), then a self-check with read-backs (one fix pass), "What I did" and one Undo; Stop between steps, then Undo or Keep. "Do it for me" on a fix or recipe card opens that plan.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/copilot.test.mjs](tests/js/copilot.test.mjs) (new, 12: schemas and persona; the example questions to steps and "Do it for me"; the model picking tools; plan → lock it in → progress → self-check → one Undo; a 3-step goal on observations; a failed tool retried or reported; Stop mid-plan; a self-check that catches a setting that didn't stick; one re-ask then the playbook; nothing outside the plan, MAX_STEPS; locations / props gather-profile-update-Undo and regenerate after a yes; characters through main's builder in a plan). Existing Ask tests pass unchanged.

**✦ Ask gets a capable brain: Ollama + Qwen3-30B-A3B bundled with the install, placed by hardware so renders keep the GPU**
- **Still wrong (read first):** never run with a real Ollama, a real Qwen3-30B-A3B or a GPU: the tiers, the unload, the proxy and the warm-up are tested with stubbed HTTP and hardware. The `qwen3:30b-a3b` tag was not re-checked live against Ollama's library. How fast it answers on the CPU tier, and whether the GPU unload finishes before a render's first model load (it runs alongside the queue, not before it), are unmeasured. The installer's Ollama step has not been run on a pod. On a fresh RunPod pod the Ollama binary (not the models) is reinstalled by `tools/start_ollama.sh`, which needs to be in the pod's start command. Ask's prompt and tools are unchanged (`ask.mjs` / `assistant.mjs` are another branch's): only the model behind them changes.
- **What:** `tools/install_runpod.sh` installs Ollama (official installer) and pulls `qwen3:30b-a3b` (`SKIP_OLLAMA=1` opts out, `SOUNDSTAGE_ASSISTANT_MODEL=qwen3:8b` pulls a smaller one); [tools/start_ollama.sh](tools/start_ollama.sh) starts it with `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1`, the App's origins and, on RunPod, `OLLAMA_MODELS=/workspace/ollama/models`. [soundstage/assistant.py](soundstage/assistant.py) picks the tier (GPU at 24 GB VRAM or more, else CPU with `num_gpu=0` at 20 GB RAM or more, else an honest message and the built-in Qwen3.5 4B), serves `GET /soundstage/assistant/status`, proxies the chat (a pod's Ollama isn't reachable from the browser), unloads the model before every render queued on the GPU tier (an on-prompt handler, so every render path is covered) and starts an installed Ollama with ComfyUI. [web/agent/director.mjs](web/agent/director.mjs): "auto" uses the bundled assistant when it's ready, else the Qwen3.5 4B; `pickOllama` prefers the assistant model; `ASSISTANT` is the hook for Ask (model, answer budget, extra system text). The Ask button's tooltip and the panel header say "warming up…" then "ready" when the App opens ([web/agent/directorpanel.mjs](web/agent/directorpanel.mjs)).
- **Tests:** [tests/test_assistant.py](tests/test_assistant.py) (new, 15: tiers for six VRAM/RAM combinations and the smaller pull, the backend decision, the status route's shape, unload before a render and not for a text job or the CPU tier, the installer's opt-outs) and [tests/js/assistant_brain.test.mjs](tests/js/assistant_brain.test.mjs) (new, 5: default backend with and without the assistant, the proxy, `pickOllama`, the warm-up).
**✦ Ask knows Soundstage: every doc, control, preset, setting, shortcut and route (offline), a troubleshooting playbook, creative recipes, and a filmmaker's voice**
- **Still wrong (read first):** never run with the real local model or in a browser: the model path is tested with canned replies, so whether Qwen3.5 4B picks `search_docs` / `troubleshoot` / `plan_recipe` well, and how well it words an answer from the chunks (a second, short round trip), is unmeasured. A question now goes to the conversation before the instant how-to recipes, so with the model on, a how-to costs a model round trip (with no model, recipes come first as before). Search is keyword BM25 (with a small stemmer), not meaning: "lock into the final" finds the Adherence fix through the playbook, but a question in other words can miss. The catalog is extracted from the code by pattern (`title:`, `aria-label`, `btn()` / `row()` / `say()` text, a label after its attrs): a label built from a template string ("Generate all missing (3)") or a variable is not in it, and "where" is the file's area (all of `web/pipeline/` is "④ Frame / ⑤ Motion / ⑥ Review"), not the exact panel. The pack is 984 KB, loaded on the first question that needs it. The playbook (9 problems) and the recipes (23 intents) are hand-written from the code's labels and the docs and were not clicked through; "Do it for me" hands a command like "apply the squib hit preset" to the Director's planner (a plan, Apply, Undo this), and whether the planner maps every one of them to its preset is not tested.
- **What** ([web/agent/knowledge.mjs](web/agent/knowledge.mjs), new; [tools/build_knowledge.mjs](tools/build_knowledge.mjs), new; [web/agent/knowledge_index.mjs](web/agent/knowledge_index.mjs), generated; [web/agent/assistant.mjs](web/agent/assistant.mjs), [web/agent/ask.mjs](web/agent/ask.mjs)): `node tools/build_knowledge.mjs` indexes README.md and docs/**/*.md (chunked by heading) with an App catalog built from the source: 506 labelled controls across `web/**`, the Presets window (`presets.json`), the SFX rigs, ⑤'s quality presets and Adherence levels (`ltx_presets.json`), the Recast engines, the "?" shortcuts and 87 `/soundstage/...` routes with their docstrings. Three new tools: `search_docs {query}` (top 5, each with where it is), `troubleshoot {symptom}` (clay not followed in the final → ⑤ Adherence Exact, the Layout IC-LoRA and Clay guide blur in Advanced (LTX), ④ Follow the clay, re-key the blocking, the Coach; characters drift or look alike; the camera clips the set; a slow or queued render; lip sync; the Ask model's cold load; a LoRA with no effect; physics; a frame that doesn't match), `plan_recipe {intent}` (stunts, squib / gunshot wound, explosion, sparks, fire, breakaway glass, camera moves, coverage, looks, match cut, dissolve, J/L cuts, montage, Ripple edit, reshoot from the timeline, Recast, phone viewfinder, record yourself). The system prompt makes Ask a collaborative director / DP / editor (one sharp creative question when an intent is vague, film-grammar options with a why, grounded in what Soundstage can do, honest about limits) and carries a short map of the 7 steps (about 400 tokens). With no model, a problem gets the playbook, a creative intent gets the recipe, any other question a how-to recipe and then the docs.
- **Automated vs clicks:** a preset does it ("Do it for me"): squib / gunshot wound, explosion (car, building), sparks, J-turn, donut, handbrake 180, crash, fall, dialogue coverage, montage (music video, trailer), Ripple edit wardrobe; the camera-clipping fix ("move the camera back"). Click-steps only: fire, breakaway glass, camera moves (one click each in the Presets window, but Ask doesn't pick one), looks, match cut, dissolve, J/L cuts, reshoot from the timeline, Recast, phone viewfinder, record yourself, and every other playbook fix.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/knowledge.test.mjs](tests/js/knowledge.test.mjs) (new, 8): the pack is fresh; 5 sample questions find their section; code-only controls (Stage-2 start, Clay guide blur, Breakable, Walk AR), a shortcut and a route; the clay-lock symptom's fix; every recipe's preset and the playbook's named controls exist; a gunshot wound is the squib rig; the persona and the map (under 1,500 tokens) in the prompt; the no-model fallback and a model turn that answers from the retrieved chunks. **✦ Ask builds a character from the script: "build the tall stranger" reads every scene he's in, profiles him with citations, writes his ② Cast card (one Undo) and, after a yes, regenerates that one sheet**
- **Still wrong (read first):** not run with the real local model or in a browser, and no GPU: the model path is tested with canned replies, so how well Qwen3.5 4B profiles from the cited evidence (cut at 3,200 characters) is unmeasured; the regenerate is tested against the real `CastPanel` methods with a stand-in generate, so FLUX 3 Klein was never called. The gather reads names only: "he" / "she" are not followed, except the sentence right after a mention in the same paragraph while it names nobody else. A description works as a name only where the script's action names the character in caps in the same sentence ("the tattooed one"). The draft profile (no model) is word lists (build, hair, face, wardrobe, a few dozen personality words) plus the dialogue's length, questions and exclamations, so "role" is counted from lines and scenes and "arc" is first and last appearance, not a reading. The lookalike check compares the new description's words with the other character cards' descriptions (≥ 40 % shared). A character without a ② Cast card (no card, e.g. not in the cast list) gets the profile but nothing is written. Locations (`gather_location` / `profile_location` / `update_location`) are not built yet.
- **What** ([web/agent/charbuild.mjs](web/agent/charbuild.mjs), new; [web/agent/assistant.mjs](web/agent/assistant.mjs), [web/agent/ask.mjs](web/agent/ask.mjs), [web/cast/cast.mjs](web/cast/cast.mjs), [web/soundstage.js](web/soundstage.js)): four new tools. `gather_character {name}` (no model) resolves a loose name ("the tall stranger", "TALL STRANGER", a typo, a first name, an alias that `check_cast` would merge, a description) and collects the scenes, dialogue, action mentions, what others say about them, wardrobe and prop cues, each `[scene:line]` (the element's place in the scene), 10 of each kind kept with counts. `profile_character {name}` hands that to the model for age, build, look, hair, wardrobe, personality, role, arc and visual notes, each with citations; a citation the evidence doesn't hold is dropped; without the model it's a cited "draft from script cues". Then it cross-checks the cast (duplicate names via `check_cast`, lookalike descriptions) and asks **Apply this to his ② Cast card?** `update_character` writes the description (what ✨ Generate paints from) and the look fields into the card; the card in chat shows before / after and **Undo** puts the card back exactly as it was. Then **Regenerate his sheet? It uses the GPU**: `regenerate_character` runs that card's own ✨ Generate (`CastPanel.generateOne`), never Generate all, and only after a yes: the model calling it is turned into the question. A yes / no to Ask's own question is answered without a model round trip. The system prompt now says Ask *is* the Soundstage assistant and lists the tools and when to use them.
- **Tests:** [tests/js/charbuild.test.mjs](tests/js/charbuild.test.mjs) (new, 7, on [a fixture script](tests/js/fixtures/script/stranger.fountain): fuzzy names, citations pointing at the right element, aliases, the cap; the cited draft; invented citations dropped; write and Undo on the real `CastPanel` methods; regenerate asks, then touches one card and never Generate all; a scripted conversation with a stubbed model: build → profile → lookalike flagged → yes → card written → yes → one sheet queued; routing ("build the set" stays a set order) and the system prompt).

**Start screen: pick a project when the App opens (Home), with thumbnails, search, and trash instead of delete**
- **Still wrong (read first):** not run in a live ComfyUI or on the pod. The screen itself was clicked through headless in Chromium with a fake server (the card grid, arrows, Enter, search, sort, the ⋯ menu, the confirm on the card, the trash call, ⌘N, Esc, phone width, and try-it mode with no server), and the library was tested with pytest on a fixture user folder. It decides "App mode" by whether the stage is on screen: ComfyUI's frontend has no App-mode flag that Soundstage reads, so a blank Soundstage_App opened in the graph view shows it too. **Import a script** into a project that isn't blank starts a New project and then asks you to choose the file in ① Script; a browser won't open a file picker after the tab reloads.
